patippuri

tamiḻmoḻiyiṉ taṉit taṉmi, toṉmi, muṉmi, teṉmi, ciṟappu ākiyavaṟṟip paṟṟi moḻiyiyalaṟiñarum piṟa tuṟiyaṟiñarum kūṟiyuḷḷavaṟṟuḷ ciṟantavaṟṟit tokuttut tarumāṟu namatu patippaka nūlāciriyarkaḷuḷ oruvarāṉa aṟiñar pi.irāmanātaṉ avarkaḷik kēṭṭuk koṇṭēṉ, avarum tokuttut tantuḷḷār. karuttukkaḷit toṭarpupaṭuttik kāṭṭum vakiyil āṅkāṅkē cila viḷakkaṅkaḷiyum tantuḷḷār. paltuṟi aṟiviyalpulaṅkaḷ cārnta vallunarkaḷ evar muṉṉiliyilum iṉṟu nilināṭṭakkūṭiya karutturikaḷē tarappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa. kūṟiyatu kūṟalāka vantaviyum, veṟum mukamaṉukkāka kūṟappaṭṭaviyum viṭappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa. mukkiyamāṉavaṟṟiyellām yār eṅku eppoḻutu ceppiyatu eṉṟa vivaraṅkaḷ tarappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa. ( āṅkila nūlkaḷ, itaḻkaḷ ākiyavaṟṟiliruntu eṭuttavaṟṟip poṟuttavari avaṟṟiṉ peyarkaḷ āṅkilattilēyē tarap paṭṭuḷḷaṉa -- mūlattit tēṭi mēlum payila virumpuvār nalaṉ karuti). ic ceytikaḷuḷ perumpālāṉavi ēṟkaṉavē namatu patippakam veḷiyiṭṭuḷḷa “toṉmic cemmoḻi tamiḻ” (2007) “tamiḻar varalāṟu: iṉṟi nōkkil - paṇṭu mutal iṉṟu vari ” (2008) ākiya nūlkaḷiliruntu tokukkappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa. cila putiya ceytikaḷum iṭam peṟukiṉṟaṉa. iṇippukaḷ I, II ākiyavi cārnta āṅkila mūlaṅkaḷukku itu varit tamiḻākkaṅkaḷ eviyum vantatākat teriyavilli. ippoḻutu mutaṉ mutalāka varukiṉṟaṉa eṉalām.

2. cila iṭaṅkaḷil (e.kā: oru pāl tamiḻukkum maṟupāl ulakiluḷḷa ērāḷamāṉa moḻikaḷ, palvēṟu moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷ ākiyavaṟṟukkum iṭiyil uḷḷa uṟavu paṟṟiya nūṟṟukkaṇakkāṉa aṟiñarkaḷ nūlkaḷip poṟuttavari avarkaḷ peyarp paṭṭiyalit tara maṭṭumē iṭamikuntatu. ivvivarattit tarum ulakappaṭam oṉṟum iyal 2- il uḷḷatu.

3. caṅka ilakkiyac ciṟappup paṟṟiya aṟiñar karuttukkaḷ iyal 4il tarappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa. icceytikaḷ moḻiyiyal cārntaviyaṉṟeṉiṉum innūlip paṭippavarkaḷ payaṉkarutic cērkkappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa;

4. innūli uruvākkiya aṟiñar pi. irāmanātaṉ avarkaḷukku eṉ naṉṟiyitterivittuk koḷkiṟēṉ.

II. 		hoyciṅṭaṉ 1853 l
“kīḻtticiyiyal āyvukaḷukkāṉa
amerikka caṅka āyvitaḻil”
(Journal of the American Oriental
Society Vol -3)  veḷiyiṭṭa tamiḻ

iyal – 1

tamiḻiya moḻik kuṭumpam

1. pirāṉcis viṭ ellis

(1816) il tamiḻiya (tirāviṭa) moḻik kuṭumpam taṉiyāṉatu eṉṟu mutalil niṟuviyavar)

“tamiḻō, teluṅkō avaṟṟiṉ iṉamoḻikaḷō camaṟkirutattiliruntu kiḷittu uruvāṉavi alla; camaṟkirutam immoḻikaḷi merukūṭṭap payaṉpaṭuttap
paṭṭirunta pōtilum avaṟṟiṉ iruppukkut (nilippukkut) tēviyaṟṟatu; avi oru taṉiyāṉa moḻik kuṭumpattic cārntavi. ammoḻikaḷil camaṟkirutac coṟkaḷ (kuṟippākap piṟkālaṅkaḷil) kalantuḷḷa pōtilum vērccoṟkaḷ niliyil avaṟṟōṭu camaṟkiruttukkut toṭarpilli. 

-Francis Whyte Ellis (1816): “Note to the introduction” (31pages) in A Grammar of the Teloogoo language by A.D.Campbell; Thomas R.Trutmann (2007): Languages and Nations : “The Dravidian proof in Colonial Madras” tamiḻil irāma.cuntaram(2007) “tirāviṭac cāṉṟu: ellisum
tirāviṭa moḻikaḷum” ceṉṉi vaḷarcci – ārāycci niṟuvaṉam ceṉṉi; maṟṟum kālaccuvaṭu patippakam; pak.340. ellis kuṟippiṉ tamiḻākkam iṇippu I l uḷḷatu. (ataṉ āṅkila vaṭivam - mūlattiṉ niḻaṟpaṭi - “tirāviṭaccāṉṟu”) iṟutiyil tarappaṭṭuḷḷatu.

2. peñcamiṉ ki pāpiṅṭaṉ (1830)

paḻantamiḻ eḻuttukkaḷip paṟṟik kūṟum poḻutu avaṟṟiṉ mikunta eḷimiyit tāṉ nāṉ mutalil kuṟippiṭa vēṇṭum. anta eḷimiyum vēṟu cila taṉmikaḷum tām tamiḻmoḻi aḷaviṟanta toṉmi vāyntatu eṉpati niṟuvukiṉṟaṉa. tamiḻi varivaṭivattiliruntu (lipiyiliruntu) uruvākkappaṭṭa kiranta lipiyil tāṉ teṉṉintiyāvil camaṟkirutam eḻutappaṭukiṟatu; kiranta lipi cikkalāṉatāka iruppatē atu piṟkālattil uruvākkappaṭṭirukka vēṇṭum eṉpataṟkuc cāṉṟākum. tamiḻmoḻi camaṟkirutattiliruntu muṟṟilum māṟupaṭṭa moḻiyamippik koṇṭatu; kaṉittu olikkum (aspirated) meykaḷ illi; camaṟkiruta neṭuṅkaṇakkil illāta olikaḷum eḻuttukkaḷum tamiḻil uḷḷaṉa. tamiḻil uḷḷa pala vaḻakkumoḻikaḷil oṉṟil (centamiḻil) camaṟkirutac coṟkaḷ mikaccilavē; tamiḻ intiyāviṉ teṉkōṭip pakutiyil iruppatu oṉṟē tamiḻ tāṉāka uruvākiyatu eṉpatiyum kuṟintapaṭcam “camaṟkirutam uruvāṉa kālak kaṭṭattilēyē uruvāṉa toṉmi uṭiyatu atu” eṉpatiyum kāṭṭuvatākum. ivviṣayam ikkaṭṭurip poruḷic cārntatalla; itip paṟṟi mēlum neṭiya viḷakkam aḷittiṭa iṅku iṭamilli.

- Benjamin Guy Babrngton(1830) An account of the sculptures and inscriptions at Mahamalipur TRANSACTIONS OF THE ROYAL ASIATIC SOCIETY.Vol II (Paper read on 12.07.1828)

3. heṉṟi hoyciṅṭaṉ (1853)

centamiḻi viṭac ceṟivu, colvaḷam, ekkaruttiyum veḷiyiṭum āṟṟal, iṉimi ākiya taṉmikaḷik koṇṭa moḻi vēṟu etuvum irukka iyalātu. 

teṉṉintiyāvil mottam ēṟattāḻa iraṇṭu mūṉṟu kōṭi pēr pēcum teluṅku, kaṉṉaṭam, maliyāḷam, tuḷuvam maṟṟum cila moḻikaḷit tōṟṟuvittatu tamiḻē eṉṟu karutappaṭukiṟatu;ākavē tamiḻit teṉṉintiyat (toṉ) moḻiyākavē karutalām.

-Reverend Henry Hoisington (1853) : Brief notes on the Tamil Language. JOURNAL OF THE AMERICAN ORIENTAL SOCIETY III Article (ix) (Paper read on 19.05.1852) itaṉ muḻumiyāṉa tamiḻākkam iṇippu II l.



4. ji.yū.pōp (1855)

“(tamiḻiya, atāvatu tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ) camaskirutam tōṉṟiya kālattil tōṉṟiyatum camaṟkirutam enta muṉmoḻiyiliruntu tōṉṟiyatō atē muṉmoḻiyi
liruntē tōṉṟiyatum āṉa oru moḻiyiliruntu uruvākip piṉ pirintavi ākum. antat tamiḻiya moḻikaḷukkum (intō āriya moḻik kuṭumpattic cārnta) kirīk, kātik, pāracīkam mutaliya moḻikaḷukkum iṭiyē oppumi iruntatik kāṭṭum pala cāṉṟukaḷ uḷḷaṉa. camaṟkirut tattukkum tamiḻiya moḻikaḷukkum oppumi illāta nērvukaḷilum kūṭa attakiya oppumikaḷ uḷḷaṉa.)” 

- tamiḻ moḻik kiyēṭu, A Hand book of Tamil Language 1855.

5. irāparṭ kālṭuvel (1856/1875): tirāviṭa allatu teṉṉintiya moḻikaḷiṉ oppilakkaṇam

nūliṉ muṉṉuriyiliruntu

tirāviṭa moḻikaḷiṉ ilakkaṇaṅkaḷi oppunōkki muṟiyāṉa āḻnta aṟiviyal pūrvamāṉa āyvi uruvākkuvatē innūlil eṉatu mukkiyamāṉa nōkka mākum. ammoḻikaḷukkum piṟamoḻikaḷukkum uḷḷa uṟavukaḷ paṟṟi iṉṟu palvēṟu karutukōḷkaḷ uḷḷaṉa veṉiṉum mēlum āyvu tēvi. vāyppu nēriṭṭa iṭaṅkaḷil ellām oru pāl tirāviṭa moḻikaḷukkum maṟupāl piṟamoḻikaḷ, moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷukkum uḷḷa uṟavi veḷippaṭuttum moḻiyiyal ilakkaṇak kūṟukaḷ, coṟkaḷ, vērkaḷ ākiyavaṟṟik kuṟippiṭṭuḷḷēṉ; kāraṇam avikaḷukkiṭiyilāṉa uṟavu paṟṟiya cikkalkaḷittīrkka itu utavum eṉṟa nampikkitāṉ.accikkalukku iṉṉum aṟiviyal pūrvamāṉa viṭi kiṭṭavilli; eṉiṉum orunāḷ kiṭṭum eṉa nampalām. ituvari viṭikaḷākak kūṟappaṭupavi veṟum karutukōḷkaḷē - ōraḷavu ēṟkattakkavi cilavum, ēṉiya cilavum. intō-airōppiya moḻikkuṭumpattukkum turāṉiya allatu skitiya moḻik kuṭumpattukkum [iṉṟu urāl - altāyk eṉappaṭuvatu] naṭuvil vikkattakkatu tirāviṭak kuṭumpam eṉpatē eṉatu muṭivu; “naṭuvil’’ eṉpati viṭa “skitiyaṉukku caṟṟu arukil ” eṉalām. tirāviṭa moḻi kaḷukkum intō-airōppiya moḻikaḷukkum iṭiyiluḷḷa moḻiyiyal, ilakkaṇaviyal uṟavukaḷum, col, vērccol uṟavukaḷum ērāḷamāṉaviyum viyakkat takkaviyumām. cila uṟupukaḷit poṟutta variyil avi taṟceyalāka nērntavi eṉṟu otukkiviṭa iyalavē iyalātu. eṉiṉum avvuṟavukaḷiṉ taṉmiyip pārkkumpoḻutu ivviru moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷukkum iṭiyilirunta uṟavu kaḻi paḻaṅkālattic cārntatākavumettaṉmi vāynta uṟavu eṉat tiṭṭavaṭṭamākak kūṟa iyalātatākavum uḷḷatu eṉpatē eṉatu karuttu.

tirāviṭa moḻik kuṭumpa moḻikaḷ intō airōppiya moḻikkuṭumpam, urāl-altāyk moḻikkuṭumpam ākiya iraṇṭu moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷukkum iṭiyē uḷḷa uṟavi viḷakkuvaṉavāka uḷḷaṉa. atu maṭṭumā? māntaṉ mutaṉmoḻi tōṉṟiya piṉṉar - am mutaṉmoḻiyiliruntu intō airōppiya moḻikkuṭumpamum , urāl-altāyk moḻik kuṭumpamum pirivataṟku muṉṉar - irunta kālattiya nilikaḷi iṉṟum kāṭṭuvaṉavāka uḷḷaṉa eṉat tōṉṟukiṟatu. patilip peyarkaḷ (Pronouns) pōṉṟavaṟṟil tirāviṭa moḻikaḷiṉ taṉmikaḷ kaḻipaḻaṅkāla nilimiyik kāṭṭuvaṉavāka uḷḷaṉa.

tamiḻiya moḻikaḷil uḷḷa cila coṟkaḷum vērkaḷum māntaṉ mutaṉ moḻiyil iruntirukkakkūṭiyavaṭivaṅkaḷilēyē iṉṟum uḷḷaṉavākak karuta iṭamuṇṭu.

nūliṉ pakkaṅkaḷ 63-79 ( III m patippu 1913) liruntu:-skitiyaṉ moḻikaḷ maṭṭumalla, airōppā, āciyā iraṇṭu kaṇṭaṅkaḷilum uḷḷa aṉittu moḻikaḷumē orē mūlattiliruntu tōṉṟiyavi eṉpatu elkiṉs muṭivu ākum. māntaṉ moḻi varalāṟṟip paṟṟi namakkut terintuḷḷa ceytikaḷ perumaḷavukku ammuṭivi ātarippaṉavākavē uḷḷaṉa. āyiṉum aṟiviyal pūrvamāka immuṭivu niṟuvappaṭa iṉṉum kālam ākum. 

tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar, intiyāvil veppa maṇṭalattil uḷḷaṉar; skitiyaṉ moḻikaḷip pēcum piṉ (Finn) makkaḷ airōppāviṉ vaṭakōṭiyilum, āstiyāk, ukriyaṉ (Ostiak, Ugrian) makkaḷ āciyāviṉ vaṭakōṭiyākiya cipīriyāvilum uḷḷaṉar. tiyūttāṉiyar(Teutons) helaṉiyar (Hellens) atāvatu kirēkkar, kelṭkaḷ Celts) ākiya intō - airōppiya moḻi pēcunar airōppāviṟkup pulam peyarntu ceṉṟu kuṭiyēṟuvataṟku muṉṉar airōppā muḻuvatum skitiyaṉ moḻi pōṉṟa (intō airōppiyamallāta) vakimoḻi pēcunarē tol kuṭikaḷāka iruntirukka vēṇṭum. anta intō airōppiya (āriya) moḻi pēcunar pulam peyarntuvantu intiyāviṟkuḷ nuḻivataṟku muṉṉart teṉṉintiyāviṉ tolkuṭikaḷāka tirāviṭar iruntirukka vēṇṭum. tirāviṭa moḻikaḷukkum intō airōppiya moḻikaḷ airōppāvil paravuvataṟku muṉṉar aṅku vaḻaṅki vanta skitiyaṉ mutaliya (intō airōppiyamallāta) moḻikaḷukkum neruṅkiya uṟavu iruppati moḻiyiyal niṟuviyuḷḷatu evvaḷavu viyakkattakkatu! varalāṟṟuk kālattukku muṟpaṭṭa uṟavākiyāl varalāṟṟiliruntu itippaṟṟi oṉṟum teriya muṭiyātu. mēmpōkkākap pārttāl ittakiya uṟavu nampamuṭiyāta putirākattāṉ tōṉṟum. “inta nilavulakam muḻuvatum paravi vāḻa aṉittu iṉa makkaḷiyum orē rattamāka karttār paṭittār” eṉṉum viviliya vācakatti itu meyppikkiṟatu.

”intō airōppiya moḻikaḷiliruntu kaṭaṉ peṟappaṭātaviyum, eṉṟālum am moḻikaḷiṉ tolpaḻaṅkālat taṉmikaḷiyottaṉavāka iruppaṉavum, āṉa piṉvaruvaṉavaṟṟi iṉṟumtirāviṭa moḻikaḷ koṇṭuḷḷaṉa. [atāvatu ,ikkāla moḻiyiyal naṭiyil kūṟuvatāṉāl, tol intō-airōppiya moḻittaṉmi kaḷit tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ tāmākavē koṇṭuḷḷaṉa.]

olippu eḷimi, iṉimikkāka ‘na’viṉ payaṉpāṭu - kirēkka moḻiyil uḷḷatu pōla
paṭarkkiyiṭap pirati peyarkaḷilum, viṉic coṟkaḷilum pāl vēṟupāṭu iruppatu - kuṟippāka potup pāl iruppatu.
cuṭṭup patilip peyarkaḷilum, paṭarkkip patilip peyarkaḷilum potuppāl orumiyik kāṭṭa d /t payaṉpāṭu.
lattīṉil uḷḷatu pōla potuppāl paṉmiyik kāṭṭa a payaṉpāṭu.
cēymic cuṭṭukku a; aṇmic cuṭṭukku i payaṉpāṭu.
perciyaṉ moḻiyiṟpōla, perumpālum iṟanta kālattik kāṭṭa t payaṉpāṭu
vēril oru oliyaṉi iraṭṭittu cila coṟkaḷil, iṟanta kālattik kāṭṭutal.
viṉic collil ōr uyireḻutti nīṭṭi olittu viṉiyālaṇiyum peyarkaḷi amittal.

“Primitive underived Indo-Europeanisms discoverable in the Dravidian Languages [in current parlance:: ‘proto-Indo European features derived from Dravidian”]

The use of n, as in Greek to prevent hiatus.
The existence of gender in the pronouns of the third person, and in verbs and in particular the existence of a neuter gender.
The use of d or t as the sign of the neuter singular of demonstrative pronouns or pronouns of the third person.
The existence of a neuter plural, as in Latin, in short a.
The formation of the remote demonstrative from a base in a; the proximate from the base in i.
The formation of most preterites, as in Persian by the addition of d
The formation of some preterites by the reduplication of a portion of the root.
The formation of a considerable number of verbal nouns by lengthening the vowel of the verbal root.

6. nallūr cuvāmi ñāṉappirakācar (1929, 1953)

“airōppiya moḻikaḷiṉ tantiyākiya toṉ moḻiyiṉ tōṟṟattiṟkāṉa ātāraṅkaḷ tamiḻiltāṉ kiṭṭum” (Tamil supplies this long looked for clue to finding the true origina of the prot Indo European language) - ceṉṉik kiṟittuvak kallūri itaḻ 1929

“intō airōppiya moḻikaḷil ‘vērkaḷ’ eṉa uṉṉikkappaṭupavi poruḷ toṭarpaṟṟa veṟum kuṟiyīṭukaḷtām. tirāviṭa moḻi vērkaḷōveṉil aiyattiṟkiṭamiṉṟip poruḷiyum kāraṇattiyum kāṭṭupaviyāka uyirōṭṭamuḷḷa muḷikkaru pōlat tōṉṟum; entak karuttōṭṭattil palvēṟu coṟkaḷ uruvāyiṉa eṉpatit teṟṟeṉa avi kāṭṭuvaṉavāka amintuḷḷaṉa. ittaṉmi avaṟṟukku illātatāl intō airōppiya ‘vērkaḷ’ iruḷ maṇṭiya poruḷaṟṟa veṟum olikkuviyalkaḷākavē tōṉṟum. avaṟṟukkum teḷivu tantu oḷikāṭṭi uyirūṭṭam tarakkūṭiyavi tirāviṭa moḻi vērkaḷtām.” - Tamil Culture 1953 [piṟamoḻi vērkaḷukkum ikkaruttu perum aḷavukkup poruntuvatākum]

7. pe. cuntaram piḷḷi (1891)

“kaṉṉaṭamum kaḷi teluṅkum kaviṉ maliyāḷamum tuḷuvum uṉ utarattu utitteḻuntē oṉṟu pala āyiṭiṉum”
- maṉōṉmaṇiyam pāyiram.

-moḻinūl (1913) pa. 86

maṟimaliyaṭikaḷ

“āriyar intiya nāṭṭiṟku kuṭipukutaṟku muṉ iv intiya nāṭu muḻutum paraviyirunta makkaḷ tamiḻarēyāvar”

“iṅṅaṉat tamiḻariṉ aṟivāḻa ārāycciyiṉi āriya naṉmakkaḷ cilar tāmum taḻuvi oḻukap pukunta kālattilē tāṉ upaniṭataṅkaḷ eḻutappaṭṭaṉa. iv upaniṭataṅkaḷ āriyarkku eṭṭātirunta aṟaṅkaḷi aṟivuṟutti, avar ceytu pōnta uyirkkoliyiṉi niṟuttutaṟporuṭṭākat tamiḻc cāṉṟōrkaḷāl iyaṟṟappaṭṭaṉavā meṉpataṟku av vupaniṭataṅkaḷilēyē maṟukkappaṭāta cāṉṟukaḷ uḷḷaṉa.”
- mullippāṭṭu ārāycciyuri (1903)

irukkuvēta kālantoṭṭē pallāyiran tamiḻc coṟkaḷ vaṭamoḻikkaṭpukuntu vaḻaṅkalāyiṉa” [vētam’ eṉṉum vaṭamoḻic col tamiḻ vērc collil uruvāṉatē eṉṟum terivittuḷḷār.] 

- tamiḻar nākarikam allatu vēḷāḷar yār? - 1923. ñā.tēvanēyap pāvāṇar

“tamiḻallāta oru moḻi tirāviṭat tāyākat tamiḻukku muṟpaṭṭu iruntatēyilli”
- tamiḻ varalāṟu ( 1967) pak.35

“tamiḻ uṇmiyil tiraviṭattāyum āriya mūlamum ākumeṉṟum aṟintu koḷka” 
- tamiḻar varalāṟu (1972) pak. 58

“intiyāvil maṭṭumaṉṟu ulakattilēyē mutaṉ mutal neṭuṅkaṇakku vakuttatum ataṟku muṟi amittatum tamiḻarē”

 “vaṭamoḻi vaṇṇamāli (alphabet) tamiḻ neṭuṅ kaṇakku muṟiyi muṟṟilum taḻuviyatu.”
- vaṭamoḻi varalāṟu (1972) pak. 300

“iraṇṭāyiram āṇṭukaḷukkum atikamāṉa varalāṟu uṭiya mikaccila ulaka moḻikaḷil tamiḻ oṉṟu. ulakiṉ pāriya moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷ oṉṟil atutāṉ, miya iṭam vakikkiṉṟatu. tirāviṭa moḻik kuṭumpam cārnta, emmoḻiyi āypavarukkum toṭakkattiliruntē tamiḻ, tamiḻ varalāṟu tamiḻiṉ paṇṭi ilakkaṇam ākiyavaṟṟit teriya vēṇṭiyatu iṉṟiyamiyātatu. tamiḻ ilakkiyac celvatti viñcum ilakkiyam ulakil vēṟu emmoḻiyilum illi. eṉavē tamiḻ ilakkiyacciṟappi ulakeṅkum aṉivarum terintu koḷḷa vaki ceyyum moḻipeyarppup paṇikaḷ tēvi.”
- ār. ī. āṣar (2004)

“tirāviṭa” moḻik kuṭumpamā? “tamiḻiya” moḻik kuṭumpamā?

moḻiyiyalil tamiḻiya (Tamulian) moḻikkuṭumpam eṉṟē kālṭuvel kālam vari vaḻaṅkiya peyari avar tāṉ “tirāviṭa” moḻik kuṭumpam eṉa māṟṟi vaḻaṅkiṉār. tamiḻ eṉpataṉ koccit tiripuvaṭivamē tirāviṭameṉpatākum. makāvamcattil “tamiḷa” , taṇṭi eḻutiya avanticuntari katiyil “tramiḷa” eṉṟa collumvarukiṉṟaṉa. ikṣvāku kuṭimarapiṉariṉ pirākirutak kalveṭṭilum ‘tamiḷa’ tāṉ varukiṟatu. piṉṉar kumārilapaṭṭariṉ tantiravārttikattil

“tatyatā tirāviṭāti pāṣāyam ēva” (appaṭi tirāviṭa (=tamiḻ) maṟṟum piṟamoḻikaḷil)

eṉṉum iṭattil tamiḻ-tamiḷa -tramiḷa-tramiṭa -traviṭa - trāviṭa eṉṟu māṟiviṭṭatu. ē.ci.parṉel 1872 intiyaṉ āṉṭikuvāri mutal tokutiyil mēṟkaṇṭa tantira vārttikap pakutiyi acciṭṭapoḻutu apattamāka,  

“āntra trāviṭa pāṣāyam ēva” 

eṉṟu acciṭṭār (mēlum paṟpala piḻikaḷuṭaṉ). intat tavaṟāṉa vācakatti kālṭuvel (1875) sṭeṉ kōṉō (Linguistic Survey of India : 1906) pōṉṟōr piṉpaṟṟiṉar. ittavaṟi pi.ṭi.cīṉivācaiyaṅkār intiyaṉ āṉṭikuvāri 42ām tokutiyil (1913) teḷḷatteḷivāka niṟuviyuḷḷār. (kuñña&ṇṇirājā Annals of Oriental Research tokuti 28 (1979) kaṭṭuriyil kuṟittuḷḷatu pōla parṉel uṭiya tavaṟāṉa vācakattit tamiḻaṟiñar palarum iṉṟum piṉpaṟṟit “tamiḻiyum, teluṅkiyum orucērak kumārilapaṭṭar kuṟittār” eṉṟu tavaṟāka eḻutivarukiṉṟaṉar.)

“tirāviṭam” eṉṉum collil iruntu “tamiḻ” uruvāṉatu eṉṟu kālṭuvel tavaṟākak kuṟippiṭṭatu paṟṟi avarik kuṟi kūṟal ollātu. akkālattil tamiḻ cārnta moḻiyiyal āyvukaḷ irunta nili avvaḷavutāṉ. āṉāl avarukkup piṉṉar 1887il kalittokip patippuriyil ci.vi.tāmōtaraṉār toṭaṅki, kamil culepil (Journal of the Institute of Asian Madras IV -2 :1987) muṭiya nūṟṟukkaṇakkāṉa moḻiyiyalaṟiñar aṉivarum “tamiḻ” eṉṉum colliṉ tiripuṟṟa vaṭivamē “tirāviṭam” eṉṟu āṇittaramāka niṟuviyuḷḷa niliyil iṉṟum “tirāviṭam” eṉṉum colliliruntu “tamiḻ” eṉṉum col uruvākiyatu eṉa kūṟikkoṇṭiruppatu aṟiyāmiyē ākum.

tirāviṭam (Proto - Dravidian) eṉṟu vaṇṇaṉi moḻi nūlāyvāḷar mīṭṭuruvākkam ceyvaṉavaṟṟuḷ pala piḻiyāy muṭikiṉṟaṉa eṉpatiyum, paḻantamiḻē itaṟkuk kaṭṭaḷik kallākak koḷḷappaṭa vēṇṭum eṉpatiyum pāvāṇar niṟuvi viṭṭār. tamiḻiṉ tiripukaḷē piṟa tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ. paḻantamiḻiṉiṉṟum vēṟupaṭṭatāka tol tirāviṭam eṉpatu kaṟpaṉiyē eṉpar pāvāṇar. ākavē tirāviṭam, tol tirāviṭam eṉa kuṟippiṭappaṭuvaṉa vellām uṇmiyil paḻantamiḻiyē kuṟippatāka uṇarka. eṉiṉum ulaka aḷavil “tirāviṭa moḻik kuṭumpam” eṉpatu oru kuṟiyīṭṭuc col ākiviṭṭa niliyil ati nām payaṉpaṭuttuvatil tavaṟilli. ataṉ uṇmip poruḷ ‘tamiḻiya moḻik kuṭumpam’ eṉpatē.

iyal – 2

tamiḻiṉ taṉittaṉmi toṉmi, muṉmi,
teṉmi, ciṟappu, ñālamutaṉ moḻikku ataṉ
nerukkam.

(kālṭuvel oppilakkaṇa nūlil iruntu eṭutta pakutikaḷ - iyal 1il kaṇṭavi- ivviyaliṉ poruṇmiyum cārntavivēyām. avarukkup piṉ vanta āyvukaḷum kuṟippākaaṇmikkāla āyvukaḷum ipporuṇmiyi valuvāka ātarippati ivviyalil uḷḷa karuttut tokuppukaḷiliruntu aṟika.)

1. hec.ār.hāl (1913)

“cumēriya uruvaccilikaḷ, puṭippuc ciṟpaṅkaḷip pārkkum poḻutu paṇṭic cumēriyar uṭalamipput tōṟṟam avarkaḷic cūḻa irunta cemittiyar, āriyar pōṉṟavarkaḷiṭamiruntu māṟupaṭṭu iruntatu - cemittiya, āriya moḻikaḷiliruntu cumēriya moḻiyum māṟupaṭṭu iruntatu pōla. cumēriyar uṭalamipput tōṟṟam intiyar pōṉṟē iruntatu. iṉṟum carācariintiyaṉuṭiya tōṟṟam āyirakkaṇakkāṉa āṇṭukaḷukku muntiya varalāṟuṭiya avaṉuṭiya tirāviṭa muṉṉōruṭiya tōṟṟam tāṉ; (intiyāviṟkuḷ vantēṟiya) āriyar uṭalamipput tōṟṟam eppoḻutō maṟintu viṭṭatu. (itu pōlavē airōppāvilum kirīs, ittāliya nāṭṭu makkaḷiṉ potuvāṉa uṭalamipput tōṟṟam annāṭukaḷil kuṭiyēṟiya āriyarkaḷuṭiyatip pōṉṟu illāmal, avarkaḷukku muntiya - airōppiya āriyarallāta - paḻaṅkuṭikaḷiṉ tōṟṟam ākavē uḷḷatu.) ākavē teṉ intiyāvil iṉṟu vacittuk koṇṭu tirāviṭa moḻi pēci varum, tirāviṭaṉ uṭalamipput tōṟṟam tāṉ cumēriyaṉ tōṟṟamum. intiyāviliruntu tari vaḻiyākavum [kaṭal vaḻiyākavum?] pāracīkam vaḻiyāka mecopotāmiyā ceṉṟu kuṭiyēṟiya (tirāviṭa) iṉattavarē cumēriyar eṉak karuta iṭamuḷḷatu...iṉṟum palūcistāṉattil tirāviṭa moḻiyāṉa pirāhuy pēcum makkaḷ uḷḷaṉar; pāracīkat teṉ pakutiyil tirāviṭar pōṉṟa uṭalamippuḷḷa makkaḷ vacikkiṉṟaṉar. pāracīkattil paṇṭu vāḻntatāka kirēkkar kūṟum “aṉāriyakō” (Anariakoi) makkaḷum (intiyāviṟkum pāpilōṉukkum iṭippaṭṭa pakutiyil paṇṭu taṅkiviṭṭa) tirāviṭarkaḷāka irukkalām. paṇṭicumēriya nakaraṅkaḷāṉa eritu pōṉṟavaṟṟukku mutaṉmutalil nākarikatti kiḻakkiliruntu pāracīka vaḷikuṭā vaḻiyāka vanta oyāṉas eṉṉum mīṉ - maṉitaṉ koṇṭu vantatāka (ki.mu. 5ām nū.cārnta) perōcas katiyiṉ uṭkaruttu eṉṉa? cumēriyāvukku kaṭal vaḻiyākavum (tirāviṭa) nākarikamceṉṟirukkalām eṉpatē atu. intiyāvil tāṉ (cintuveḷiyil?) tirāviṭanākarikam mutalil vaḷarntirukka vēṇṭum. aṅku tāṉ eḻuttukku varivaṭivam (Script) mutaṉ mutalil kaṇṭupiṭikkappaṭṭu, mutalil paṭaveḻuttāka iruntu piṉṉar tiruntiya eḷiya vari vaṭivam uruvākkappaṭṭu, cumēriyāvukkum paravi aṅku nāḷaṭivil kuṉēpārm āppeḻuttu uruppeṟṟirukka vēṇṭum. cumēriyā cellum vaḻiyil tirāviṭar elām pakutiyilum taṅkaḷ nākarikatti viṭṭuc ceṉṟirukka vēṇṭum. eṉavē cumēriyāvukku nākarikattip parappiyatākak kūṟappaṭum (cemittiyarum allāta, āriyarum allāta) putiyavarkaḷ intiyāviliruntu aṅku ceṉṟa tirāviṭarāka irukkalām. āṉāl itaṟku nēraṭi ātāram illi. eṉavē iti oru muṟṟāṉa muṭivāka alla; oru karutukōḷāka nāṉ muṉ vikkiṟēṉ.”

-H.R. Hall (1913) Ancient History of the Near East mecapotāmiyāvil kuṭiyēṟiya tamiḻarē cumēriya nākarikatti uruvākkiyirukkalām eṉṉum ikkarutti pi.ṭi.cīṉivāca aiyaṅkār tamatu History of the Tamils (1929) nūlil ēṟṟuḷḷār. kāntiratāv (The Riddles of the Three Oceans; (1974) tamiḻākkam “tamiḻa nākarikamum cintuveḷi nākarikamum: teṉ peruṅkaṭal āyvukaḷ(2007)) avarkaḷukkum ikkaruttu ēṟpuṭiyatē.


2. es.heṉṟi hīrās

speyiṉ nāṭṭic cārnta tirāviṭar eṉṟu taṉṉi aḻittuk koṇṭa es.hīrās pātiriyār (11.09.1888 - 14.12.1955) toṉmi intō -naṇṇilakkari nākarika āyvu (Studies in Proto-Indo Mediterranean Culture 1953) eṉṉum nūlil cintuveḷi nākarikam tirāviṭaruṭiyatu; avarkaḷ moḻi tirāviṭa moḻi eṉpati niṟuviyuḷḷār. mikap paḻaṅkālattil (ki.mu.5000kkum neṭuṅkālattukku muṉṉar) mutaṟkaṇ kāvirikkariyil uruvākiya arappā nākarikam intiya mēṟkuk kari vaḻiyākat tamiḻarkaḷāl cintuveḷi, cumēriyā, ekiptu, naṇṇilakkari nāṭukaḷ vari koṇṭu cellappaṭṭatu eṉpatu hīrās koḷki. [avar vātattukku ātāramākak koṇṭa ceytikaḷ cila ippoḻutu tavaṟākat tōṉṟalām; cintuveḷi muttirikaḷi avar tirāviṭa moḻi cārntavi eṉṟu coṉṉa muṭivu cariyeṉṟālum avar muttirikaḷil paṭittuk kaṇṭa vācakaṅkaḷ iṉṟu ēṟkattakkaṉavāka illi. iruntālum avar kaṇṭa aṭippaṭi uṇmi iṉṟum valuvuṭiya tākavē uḷḷatu]

3. eṉ.lakōvāri

i) 6000 āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar intiyāviliruntu aṭlāṇṭik kaṭalvari orē mātiriyāṉa “pala col piṇippu oṭṭunili” (Polysynthetic suffixal) moḻikaḷ, iṭiyīṭu iṉṟip paraviyiruntaṉa. avaṟṟiṉ coṟkaḷañciyaṅkaḷ ōraḷavukku oṉṟupōl iruntaṉa.potuccol viḻukkāṭu moḻikku moḻi māṟupaṭṭu iruntirukkalām. tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ, elām, cumēriyaṉ, harri, kākēciyaṉ, halti, pōṉṟavi orē moḻiyamippuk koṇṭu, oṉṟukkoṉṟu uṟavuṭiyaviyākac caṅkilit toṭar pōla amintiruntaṉa.

ii) intō airōppiya moḻi pēcunar (ki.mu.2000ai oṭṭi) airōppāvukkuḷ kiḻakkiliruntu nuḻiyumuṉṉar aṅku pēcappaṭṭu vanta moḻikaḷil oṉṟāṉa pāsku (yūskarā) iṉṟum pirāṉcukkum speyiṉukkum iṭippaṭṭa pireṉīs malippakutiyil pēcappaṭṭu varukiṟatu. tol tirāviṭa moḻi inta pāsku moḻiyuṭaṉ maṭṭumiṉṟi intō airōppiya moḻikaḷukku muṉṉar airōppāvil pēcappaṭṭa toṉ moḻikaḷuṭaṉum uṟavuṭiyatu.

iii) immoḻikaḷ aṉittum oṉṟukkoṉṟu neruṅkiya toṭarpuḷḷavi; orē potuvāṉa tāy moḻiyiṉ vaṭṭāra vaḻakkukaḷ eṉṟē kūṟalām.

- N. Lahovary (1963) tirāviṭar tōṟṟamum mēl nāṭukaḷum (Dravidian origins and the west) “tirāviṭar naṇṇilakkari nāṭukaḷiliruntu pulam peyarntu vantu intiyāvil cintuveḷiyil mutaṟkaṇ kuṭiyēṟip piṉṉar kumari varip paraviṉar” eṉṟa apattak koḷkiyi avar (vivaram puriyāmal) piṉpaṟṟiyirunta pōtilum innūl ciṟanta moḻiyiyal meymmikaḷi uṇarttuvatākum.

4. piriṭjaṭ & rēmaṇṭ ālciṉ (1988)

“teṉṉintiyā iṉṟu tirāviṭamoḻikaḷ vaḻaṅkum pakuti. ammoḻikaḷip pēcuvōr pūrvīkam eṉṉavāka iruntirukkalām? avarkaḷ (vaṭamēṟkiliruntu) teṉṉintiyāviṟku vantu kuṭiyēṟumpōtu tammuṭaṉ irumpiyum peruṅ kaṟpaṭi nākarikattiyum koṇarntaṉar eṉa (valuvāṉa ātāra miṉṟik) kūṟappaṭukiṟatu. tolliyal cāṉṟukaḷiṉpaṭi ippaṭi naṭantirukka vāyppu mikak kuṟivu. irumpup payaṉ pāṭṭiṟku 1000 āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar iruntē karunāṭakattil (teṉ intiyāvil piṟa pakutikaḷilum kūṭa irukkalām) peruṅkaṟpaṭi nākarikak kuṭiyiruppukaḷ iruntuḷḷaṉa;avi iṭiyīṭu iṉṟit toṭarntu iruntu vantuḷḷaṉa; viyakkat takka paṇpāṭṭut toṭarcciyuṭaṉ iṉṟuma uḷḷa paṇpāṭṭuk kūṟukaḷ pala antap paḻiya nākarikak kūṟukaḷiṉ toṭarcciyākavē tōṉṟukiṉṟaṉa. uṭaṟkūṟu amippilum antap paḻiya nākarika makkaḷum iṉṟiya makkaḷum perum aḷavukku oṉṟu pōlavē uḷḷaṉar. eṉavē teṉṉintiyāvil (pallāyiram āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar iruntē) putukkaṟkāla nākarikam paṭitta makkaḷiṭiyē uruvāṉaviyē tirāviṭamoḻikaḷ eṉpati maṟuppatu kaṭiṉamāka uḷḷatu. teṉṉintiyāvil paṟpala pakutikaḷil irunta putukkaṟkāla nākarika makkaḷiṭiyē toṭarpu iruntirukkum; potuvāṉa tirāviṭa moḻiyum iruntirukkum. ki.mu.3000-1000 kāla aḷavil teṉṉintiyāvil irunta putuk kaṟkāla kuṭiyiruppukaḷil iruntu piṟantaviyē varalāṟṟuk kālat toṭakkattic cārnta kuṭiyiruppukaḷum eṉalām.

The southern part of the peninsula is today the homeland of the Dravidian Language, and we may well enquire what- speaking in broadest terms-is likely to have been their history. It has been climed, though not on very solid grounds, that the earliest speakers of these languages brought with them into Peninsular India both Iron and the custom of making megalithic graves. In the light of archeological evidence this appears to be extremely improbale. We now know that atleast for a millennium prior to the arrival of iron, there were established settlements in Karnataka, and probably also in other parts of the peninsula, and these settlements show evidence of a remarkable continuity of culture. Many modern culture trits appear to derive from them, and a substantial part of the populations shows physical affilation to the Neolithic people. In the Light of all this it is difficult to believe that the Dravidian languages do not owe their origin to the same people who produced the Neolithic culture there. xxxx the several regional cultures.... had throughout a degree of interaction and probably originally a common language family, Dravidian. xxxx the settlements of the third - second millennia appear to be ancestral to those which we encounter there from the beginnings of history
onwards (p.353) 

- Bridget and Raymond Allchin (1988) The rise of Civilisation in India and Pakistan

rikvētam tokukkappaṭṭa kālam ki.mu. 1500 - 1300ākum. intō āriya moḻip paṭippukaḷil mikappaḻiyatu atuvē. avvētattilēyē intō- āriyamallātaṉavum, tirāviṭat taṉmi vāyntaṉavumāṉa pala kūṟukaḷ – oliyaṉ māṟṟaṅkaḷ, tirāviṭattiliruntu kaṭaṉpeṟṟa coṟkaḷ, iṭappeyarkaḷ, makkaṭ peyarkaḷ ākiyavi uḷḷaṉa. ki.mu.1500kku muṉṉar cila nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷ kālam vaṭamēṟku intiyāvil vētamoḻi pēciyavarkaḷum tirāviṭa moḻi pēciyavarkaḷum neruṅkiya paṇpāṭṭut toṭarpu koṇṭu vāḻntirukka vēṇṭum eṉpati itu kāṭṭukiṟatu.
- mēlatu

The earliest Indo - Aryan text, the compiled Rg Veda, shows several influences of a non -Indo - Aryan, Dravidian element in the form of phonetic changes. introduction of loan words and names etc. These presuppose the coexistence of the Vedic and Dravidian speaking peoples in a cultural contact situtation for a period, perhaps of centuries, before the compilation of the Rg Veda (circa 1500 - 1300 BC)

[aṭuttu varum pakkaṅkaḷil “puṭaviyiṉ tōṟṟatti liruntu iṉṟiya māntaṉ vari” eṉṉum viḷakkap paṭam tarappaṭṭuḷḷatu. mānta iṉat tōṟṟam , moḻiyiṉ tōṟṟam pōṉṟavi kuṟittu palvēṟu aṟiviyaṟ pulaṅkaḷil iṉṟiya aṟiñarkaḷ ēṟkum muṭivukaḷi atu kāṭṭukiṟatu. ]

5. ñāla mutaṉmoḻi kuṟittum ataṟkut tamiḻ mika neruṅkiya teṉpatu kuṟittum aṇmikkālattil uruvākiyuḷḷa karuttōṭṭaṅkaḷ:

(1) iṉṟu ulakil pēcappaṭum 6000 moḻikaḷum palvēṟu moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷic cērntaviyāka vakip paṭuttappaṭṭaḷḷaṉa. akkuṭumpaṅkaḷuḷ mukkiyamāṉavi intō-airōppiyam, tirāviṭam, urāl-altāyk, āprō āciyaṉ (hamitō - cemittik) ākiyaviyākum. innāṉku moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷumē potuvāṉa oru moḻip peruṅkuṭumpattiliruntu Macrofamily tōṉṟiyirukka vēṇṭum eṉṟu 1900 ai oṭṭi airōppiya moḻiyilaṟiñar cilar karutiṉar. ammoḻip peruṅ kuṭumpattukku 1904-il ṭeṉmārk aṟiñar hālter petarcaṉ nāstirātik eṉṟu peyar cūṭṭiṉār. lattīṉ moḻiyil nāstirās eṉṟāl nammavaṉ eṉṟu poruḷ. accolliliruntu nāstirātik (namatumoḻi) eṉṟa colli petarcaṉ uruvākkiṉār; ivvāyvukaḷik kuṟittiṭa nāstirātiks eṉṉum col uruvākkap paṭṭuḷḷatu. 1960kaḷil raciya moḻiyiyal aṟiñar ilic cuvitic ittuṟiyil virivāṉa āyvukaḷ ceytār. antō! avar 1965il untu vipattil iṟantu viṭṭār. nāstirātik tavira ēṉiya moḻip peruṅkuṭumpaṅkaḷāka moḻiyiyalaṟiñar iṉṟu ēṟṟuḷḷa ēṉiya moḻip peruṅkuṭumpaṅkaḷ varumāṟu: cīṉa - kākēciyaṉ, āstirik, amerikki ntiyaṉ, intō-pacipik, kōycāṉ, kāṅkō-cakārā

2. petarcaṉ, cuvitic ākiyōr niṟuviya nāstirātik, amerikkar kirīṉperku 2000il niṟuviya yūrēciyāṭik ivviraṇṭukkum iṭiyē atika vēṟupāṭu illi. kāṇka:

1. nāstirātik moḻip peruṅkuṭumpattil aṭaṅkiya moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷ:
1965il cuvitic karuttuppaṭi:

intō-airōppiya moḻikaḷ, urālik (piṉṉiṣ mutaliyavi), altāyik (turukkiyam mutaliyavi), tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ, kārṭveliyaṉ (jārjiya moḻi mutaliyavi) āprō-āciyaṉ (cemittiya moḻikaḷum hāmittiya moḻikaḷum)

2. piṟṟi nāstirātik āyvāḷar karuttuppaṭi:

āprō-āciya moḻikaḷi nīkkuka:- stārōstiṉ, cevarōskiṉ, alekcis māṉāsṭar rēmar
cukōtiyaṉ, eskimō-ālyūt cērkka : tālkōpālski;
cappāṉiyam, koriyam cērkka (altāyik kuṭumpattil allatu taṉiyāka) pāmhārṭu, stārōskiṉ
kārṭveliyaṉi nīkkuka:- pāmhārṭu, kerṉs
elāmiṭṭi cērkka:- tālkōpālski
cumēriyattic cērkka:- pāmhārṭu

(2) kirīṉ perk-iṉ yūrēciyāṭik moḻip peruṅkuṭumpattil aṭaṅkiyavi;-

etraskaṉ, intō-airōppiya moḻikaḷ, urālik - yūkākir, altāyik, koriyaṉ-jappāṉiyam-jaṉu, cukōtiyaṉ, eskimō-alyūt.

3. rōjar piḷeñc & mātyū spiriks patippitta tolliyalum moḻiyum (1997/1999 ruṭleṭj) nūl nāṉku maṭalaṅkaḷ koṇṭatu. nāstirātik moḻip peruṅkuṭumpattil tirāviṭa moḻikaḷiṉ iṭam kuṟittu iraṇṭu vēṟupaṭṭa karuttukkaḷi annūlil kāṇalām:-

tol nāstirātik

(tirāviṭa moḻikkum elām moḻikkum iṭiyilāṉa oppumiyi viṭa elām moḻikkum āprō āciyāṭik, tolcumēriyaṉ moḻikaḷukkum iṭiyē irunta oppumi nerukkamāṉatu eṉpar pilācak)

4. sṭāṉpōrṭu palkalikkaḻakattil 2000 akṭōpar mātam patrirāju kiruṣṇamūrttiyiṭam pēcumpoḻutu kirīṉperku “tirāviṭa moḻik kuṭumpam yūrēciyāṭikkiṉ uṭaṉpiṟappāka (cakōtariyāka) irukka muṭiyumēyoḻiya makaḷāka irukka muṭiyātu” eṉṟār. (kiruṣṇamūrtti The Dravidian Languages 2003 pak.46) itaṉ poruḷ eṉṉa? intō airōppiya moḻik kuṭumpattil piṟanta uṭkuṭumpaṅkaḷē slāv, irāṉiyaṉ; vētamoḻi; camaṟkirutam, kirīk, lattīṉ, kelṭik, cerumāṉiyam mutaliyavi. tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ ivaṟṟukku maṭṭumalla, ivaṟṟiṉ tāyāṉa, intō-airōppiya moḻikkuṭumpattukkum muntiya paḻami vāyntatu eṉpatē kirīṉperk karuttu.
5. nāstirātik toṉmoḻikkum muntiyatākiya ñālamutaṉmoḻi (Mother Tongue of man allatu Proto- World) paṟṟi iṉṟu moḻiyiyalaṟiñar kōṭpāṭu eṉṉa? aṭlāṉṭik maṉtli eṉṉum amerikka itaḻil (1991 ēpral: pak.39-68) rāparṭ riṭ eṉpavar itupaṟṟi nayampaṭa eḻutiyuḷḷār. attakiya ñāla mutaṉmoḻi cārnta āyvukaḷil iṉṟu īṭupaṭṭuḷḷavarkaḷ meriṭ rūlaṉ, jāṉ peṅṭcaṉ, vāklāv pilācak, vitāli cevarōṣkiṉ (Merrit - Ruhlen, John Bengtson, Vaclav Blazek, Vitaly Shevoroshkin) pōṉṟavarkaḷāvar. yūrēciyāṭik paṟṟi kirīṉperk iṟappataṟku muṉ kaṭiciyāka eḻutiya nūl intō-airōppiya moḻikaḷum avaṟṟōṭu neruṅkiya uṟavuṭiyaṉavum - yūrēciyāṭik moḻip peruṅ kuṭumpam. Indo-European and its closest relaties: the Eurasiatic Language Family: Vol 1; Grammar; Vol II: Lexicon” (sṭāṉ pōrṭu palkalikkaḻaka accakam, 2000/2002) eṉpatākum.

6. palvēṟu moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷukkiṭiyēyuḷḷa uṟavu (atāvatu tol paḻaṅkālattila avi evvāṟu oṉṟāka iruntu paṭippaṭiyākap pirintu oṉṟu – palavāki yirukkalām eṉpatu) paṟṟi eḻutum moḻiyiyalāḷarkaḷiṭiyē ār.el.tirāsk (R.L.TraskHistorical Linguistics; Arnold, London 1996) uṭiya piṉvarum karuttē viruppu veṟuppaṟṟatum ciṟantatum ākum

“tol-nāstirātik cumār 15000-10000 āṇṭu kaḷukku muṉṉar pēcappaṭṭirukkalām, moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷiṉ kālvaḻi paṟṟiya karutukōḷkaḷilēyē nāstirātik tāṉ perumaḷavu ēṟkat takkatāyuḷḷatu. iṉṉum pattāṇṭukaḷil (tirāsk iti eḻutiyatu 1996il)nāstirātik koḷki palarālum ēṟkappaṭalām”

(tirāviṭa, urāl-altāyk, cappāṉiya moḻikaḷ 10000 āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉarp pirintirukkalām eṉpar cuvelapil (1990) (Dravidian Linguistics - an Introduction.)

7. iṉṟu ulakil uḷḷa 660 kōṭi makkaḷumē hōmō cēpiyaṉs allatu “taṟkāla uṭalamippu māntar” (Anatomically Modern Humans) eṉṉum orē iṉattic cērntavarkaḷ eṉpatum avviṉam cumār oṉṟari ilaṭcam āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar āppirikkāvil tōṉṟiyatu eṉpatum, moḻi eṉpatu 50,000 āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉart tōṉṟiyirukkalām eṉpatum palvēṟu aṟiviyal tuṟi viññāṉikaḷiṉ iṉṟiya orumitta muṭivākum. mutal tāymoḻi tōṉṟiya ēṟattāḻa aimpatāyiram āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉar iruntutāṉ, taṟkāla mānta iṉam virintu ulakiṉ aṉittuk kaṇṭaṅkaḷukkum paravuvataṟkāṉa cūḻnili ēṟpaṭṭirukkalām eṉpatum, ap paraval teṉ intiyā vaḻiyāka naṭipeṟṟirukkalām eṉpatum avarkaḷ karuttākum. tol tamiḻ (tirāviṭa) tōṟṟamāṉatu moḻi uruvākiyatum taṟkāla mānta iṉam ulakeṅkum paravat toṭaṅkiyatumāṉa aimpatāyiram āṇṭut toṉmi uṭiyatākalām.( Christiansen M.H.et.al (2003) Language Evolution:Colin Masica (2001) etc..

intumākkaṭalil intiyāvukkut teṟkilirunta teṉṉāṭṭup pakutikaḷ paṇṭu kaṭalkoḷḷappaṭṭut tamiḻakap parappu curuṅkiyatākap paḻantamiḻ ilakkiyaṅkaḷum marapuc ceytikaḷum kūṟuvatu ēṟattāḻa ki.mu.8000ai oṭṭiyum piṉṉarum ulakeṅkum kaṇṭattiṭṭup pakutikaḷ (Continential shelf) kaṭaluḷ mūḻkiyatiyē kuṟippiṭuvatākak koḷvatu maṭṭumē iṉṟiya aṟiviyalukku ottatu eṉpati irāmanātaṉ Dravidian studies (tirāviṭap palkalikkaḻakam, kuppam) 2003 kaṭṭuriyil kāṇka. aṭuttuḷḷa paṭam mānta iṉapparavalilum moḻiyiṉ paravalilum teṉṉintiyāvum, tamiḻakamum iṉṟikku 50,000 āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉar vakitta mukkiya paṅkik kāṭṭuvatākum:-

8. aṇmiyil moḻiyiyalaṟiñar stepāṉ hilyar leviṭ tamatu 2007 kaṭṭuriyil mēṟkaṇṭa kōṭpāṭaṭi ātarikkiṟār.

“kaṭal maṭṭam ippoḻutuḷḷativiṭa mikak kuṟivāka irunta paṉiyūḻik kālattil āppirikkā – teṉṉintiyāvi iṇitta vāl pōṉṟa nila iṇippukaḷ/tīvukaḷ vaḻiyāka āppirikkāviliruntu teṉ āciyāviṟku tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar vantirukkalāmeṉṉum kōṭpāṭṭi pi.irāmanātaṉ valiyuṟuttukiṟār. ak kōṭpāṭṭināṉum ātarikkiṟēṉ. āstirēliyap paḻaṅkuṭi makkaḷip paṟṟi āyvuceyta aṟiñar ammakkaḷiṉ moḻikaḷ tirāviṭa moḻikaḷuṭaṉ

ammakkaḷukkum tirāviṭarukkum uḷḷa toṭarpik kāṭṭukiṉṟaṉa. ki.mu.6000ai oṭṭi ulakeṅkum kaṭal maṭṭam uyarntu, nilappakutikaḷiṉ parappu curuṅkiya poḻutu ulakiṉ piṟa pakuti makkaḷuṭiya nākarikaṅkaḷiṭam iruntu tuṇṭikkappaṭṭaṉar āstirēliyap paḻaṅkuṭi makkaḷ. avarkaḷ teṉṉintiyāviliruntu āstirēliyāvukkup pulam peyarntatu ēṟattāḻa 40,000āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉar irukkalām eṉkiṉṟaṉar āstirēliya aṟiñar.itiliruntu kuṟintatu 40,000 āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉarē tirāviṭar intiyāvil iruntaṉar eṉpatu teḷivākiṉṟatu. inta [“tirāviṭar ēṟṟam”] kōṭpāṭṭiṉpaṭi tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar teṉṉintiyāviliruntu vaṭanāṭu celkiṉṟaṉar; piṉṉar aṅkiruntu pāracīkattiṟkum atit tāṇṭip piṟa nilappakutikaḷukkum celkiṉṟaṉar. anta piṟa nilappakutikaḷil mūla tirāviṭa moḻik kuṭumpattiliruntu pirintu urālik, altāyik, intō-airōppiyam ākiya moḻikkuṭumpaṅkaḷ uruvākiṉṟaṉa. intō-airōppiya moḻik kuṭumpattukku tirāviṭam tāyā? tamakkiyā? eṉpati iṉṟiya niliyil tiṭṭa vaṭṭamākak kūṟa iyalātu”
- International Journal of Dravidian Linguistics; June 2007

ōppaṉ himar 2003il eḻutiya The Real Eve: Modern Man’s Journey out of Africa Carroll and Graff Publishers, New york  nūliṉpaṭi marapaṇu āyvum intiyāviliruntu vaṭakku vaṭamēṟkāka ēṟattāḻa 50000 āṇṭukku muṉṉar miya kiḻakku- miya āciyā –airōppā eṉṟavāṟu paṭippaṭiyākap paraviyirukkalām eṉṟa kōṭpāṭṭiṟku ātaravāka uḷḷatu eṉpatukuṟippiṭattakkatu.

(9) mika aṇmiyil tāṉ, mutaṉ mutalāka mēṉāṭṭu moḻiyiyalaṟiñar pāvāṇar kōṭpāṭukaḷi ātarikkum karuttukkaḷi veḷiyiṭṭuḷḷaṉar; kāṇka.

1. stepāṉ hilyar leviṭ: “intō airōppiya moḻikaḷukkum tirāviṭa moḻikaḷukkum iṭiyēyuḷḷa toṭarpukaḷ” (intō airōppiya moḻiyiyal āyvitaḻ) maṭalam 28: eṇ.3,4 cūṉ ṭica.2000 pak.407-438)pāvāṇar tamatu “The Primary classical language of the world(1966)” nūlil tamiḻ moḻiyiliruntu intō airōppiya moḻikaḷiṟ pukuntuḷḷaṉavākak kuṟippiṭuvaṉavaṟṟuḷ nūṟṟukku mēṟpaṭṭavi ēṟkattakkaṉaveṉpatu ivar karuttu.

2) tamatu (2000) nūlil kirīṉperk yūrēciyāṭik peruṅkuṭumpattiṉ mukkiyamāṉa ilakkaṇa oppumikaḷ 72-aik kuṟippiṭṭuḷḷār. anta 72 iṉaṅkaḷil 20-kku mēṟpaṭṭavaṟṟukkut tirāviṭa moḻikaḷiṉ ilakkaṇak kūṟukaḷum porunti varukiṉṟaṉa.

3) hec.pi.ē.hakōlā (piṉlāntu) (i) āyiram turāljaṉ (DURALJAN) vērccoṟkaḷ (2000); innūlil tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ, urāl-altāyk iṉa moḻikaḷ, cappāṉiya moḻi, teṉ amerikka perunāṭṭu koṣuvā moḻi, ākiyavaṟṟiṭiyē 1000 aṭippaṭi oppumic coṟkaḷit tantuḷḷār.

4) cumēriya moḻiyum tolturāljaṉ moḻikaḷum (2003): hajat acātiyaṉ uṭaṉ iṇintu eḻutiyatu. muntiya nūliṟ kaṇṭa 1000 oppumic coṟkaḷil 410 coṟkaḷ (ki.mu.3500-1800 kālakkaṭṭattic cārnta) cumēriya moḻic coṟkaḷōṭum ottiruppati niṟuvukiṟatu:

10) tirāviṭa moḻikaḷukkum piṟa moḻikaḷukkum iṭiyēyuḷḷa uṟavukaḷ kuṟittu ituvari nikaḻntuḷḷa āyvukaḷiṉ paṭṭiyal varumāṟu:-

11) ñālamutaṉmoḻi āyvāḷar meriṭ rūlaṉ taṉatu moḻikaḷiṉ tōṟṟam - moḻikaḷiṉ koṭi vaḻi āyvu sṭāṉpōrṭu palkalikkaḻakam, 1994 nūliṉ pakkam 277 il kūṟuvatu varumāṟu:

 “perumpālāṉa moḻiyiyalaṟiñarkaḷ ottuk koḷḷāviṭiṉum allatu aiyappāṭṭuṭaṉ karutiṉum iṉṟuḷḷa moḻikaḷ aṉittum (iṉṟu iṟantuviṭṭa āṉāl muṉṉarnilaviyataṟkuc cāṉṟukaḷ kiṭṭiyuḷḷa moḻikaḷ uṭpaṭa) orē ñāla mutaṉ moḻiyiliruntu tāṉ tōṉṟiṉa eṉpataṟku valuvāṉa ātāram uḷḷatu.”

annūliṉ pakkaṅkaḷ 277-366il 27 mukkiyamāṉa karuttu kaḷukku palvēṟu moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷilum uḷḷa coṟkaḷ “Global Etymologies” tarappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa. akkaruttukaḷukku ñāla mutaṉmoḻiyil eṉṉa vērccol iruntirukkalām eṉṟu ūkikka atu muṉṉōṭiyākum. avaṟṟuḷ palavaṟṟukku (ēṟattāḻa 17kku) t tamiḻc coṟkaḷē poruḷ potintaṉavākavum ñālamutaṉmoḻiyiṉ vērc col vaṭivi viḷakkuvaṉavākavum amintuḷḷaṉa; “tamiḻē iyaṉ moḻi’ eṉap pāvāṇar ceppiyati itu meyppikkiṟatu)

“perumpālāṉa moḻiyiyalaṟiñarkaḷ ottuk koḷḷā viṭiṉum allatu aiyappāṭṭuṭaṉ karutiṉum iṉṟuḷḷa moḻikaḷ aṉittum (iṉṟu iṟantuviṭṭa āṉāl muṉṉar nilaviyataṟkuc cāṉṟukaḷ kiṭṭiyuḷḷa moḻikaḷ uṭpaṭa) orē ñāla mutaṉmoḻiyiliruntu tāṉ tōṉṟiṉa eṉpataṟku valuvāṉa ātāram uḷḷatu”.

13. centamiḻc celvi oṉpatām maṭalam (1931-32) 308m pakkattil pāvāṇar ‘vaṭamoḻic ceṉṟa teṉ coṟkaḷ’ kaṭṭuriyil “ettaṉiyō teṉcoṟkaḷ vaṭamoḻic ceṉṟu vākukarāy vaḻaṅki varukiṉṟaṉa. avi naḷarākum kālamum naṇṇum” eṉṟār. akkaṭṭuri vaṭamoḻi paṟṟiyatu maṭṭumē yātaliṉ avvāṟu kūṟiṉār. piṉṉar aṭutta 50 āṇṭukaḷil intō, airōppiya moḻikaḷ, piṟamoḻik
kuṭumpam cār moḻikaḷ ākiyavaṟṟilum mikappala tamiḻc coṟkaḷ ēṟiyuḷḷatip pāvāṇarē niṟuviyuḷḷārākaliṉ,  “vaṭamoḻic ceṉṟu” eṉpati “piṟamoḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷiṭic ceṉṟu” eṉak koḷvatu poruttam. ñālattiṉ palvēṟu moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷiṭic ceṉṟu vākukarāy vaḻaṅkum tamiḻc coṟkaḷ naḷarākum kālam. (atāvatu pāvāṇariṉ kaṉavu naṉavākum kālam) naṇṇiviṭṭatu eṉalām.

14. aṇmiyil cemmoḻi ak-tica2007 itaḻil stepāṉ leviṭ ñālamutaṉmoḻi cār āyvukaḷil tirāviṭa moḻikaḷiṉ mukkiyattuvattip piṉvarumāṟu valiyuṟuttiyuḷḷār:-

nāstirātik āyvukaḷi 1960-70 kaḷil toṭaṅkip perum aḷavil vaḷarac ceytavarkaḷ (raciyar) vilātislāv illic - cuvitic, intiyāvil tēvanēyaṉ ākiyōrāvar. palkalikkaḻaka nili moḻiyiyal tuṟikaḷil kōlōcciyavarkaḷ, “nāstirātik” āyvukaḷit tīṇṭattakātaṉa vākak karutiya kālam atu. illic-cuvitic, tēvanēyaṉ iruvarum ittuṟiyil uṇmi oḷi kāṇa vaḻi kāṭṭiya muṉṉōṭikaḷ. palvēṟu moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷi iṇittu, “moḻip peruṅkuṭumpaṅkaḷ Superfamilies āka vakip paṭuttic ceyyum āyvukaḷip palkalikkaḻaka nili moḻiyiyalaṟiñarum ēṟkum nili iṉṟu uruvākiyuḷḷatu. attakiya āyvukaḷil mutaliṭam peṟa vēṇṭiya tirāviṭa moḻikaḷiṉ mukkiyattuvam iṉṉum uṇarappaṭavilli.. tamiḻc cērccoṟkaḷiṉ aṭippaṭiyil tēvanēyaṉ muṉ vittuḷḷa moḻiyiyal vātaṅkaḷ palavum nāstirātik pōṉṟa āyvukaḷil tirāviṭamoḻikaḷ vakikkum mukkiya iṭatti viḷakkukiṉṟaṉa. attakiya āyvukaḷil ‘tirāviṭa intō airōppiya” col uṟavu āyvum oṉṟu; avaṟṟuḷ mukkiyamāṉatum kūṭa” 
moḻiyiyal cārnta aṟiñar cilar karuttukaḷ (ivviyalōṭu toṭarpuṭiyavi)

6. “intiya oru moḻiyiyaṟpulam” āyvāḷarkaḷ tirāviṭa moḻikaḷiruntu intō āriya moḻikaḷukkuc ceṉṟuḷḷaṉavākak karutum ilakkaṇak kūṟukaḷum, moḻiyiyal kūṟukaḷum

intiya moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷiṭiyē potumi kuṟittu mutaṉ mutalil virivāka 1956 il (Language, 32; pak 2-16) “intiyā oru moḻiyiyaṟ pulam India as a Linguistic Area  eṉṉum kaṭṭuriyil eḻutiyavar emeṉō intiyāvil uḷḷa tamiḻiya, intō āriya, muṇṭā iṉa moḻikaḷiṭiyē potumik kūṟukaḷ (perum pālāṉavi tamiḻiya moḻikaḷiṭamiruntu piṟavaṟṟukkup paraviyavi) uḷḷati akkaṭṭuri niṟuviyatu. ikkōṭ pāṭṭiṉ karu veku muṉṉarē uruvāṉatu. (1788lēyē carvilliyam jōṉs inti mutaliya vaṭa intiya moḻikaḷiṉ ilakkaṇakkūṟukaḷ intō āriyamoḻik kuriyaviyalla [appoḻutu “tamiḻiya moḻik kuṭumpam” paṟṟi mēṉāṭṭaṟiñarukku teriyātu] eṉṟu kūṟiyuḷḷār:

(Regarding modern Indo - Aryan Languages) : “and this analogy might induce us to believe, that the pure Hindi, whether of Tartarian or Chaldean origin, was primeval in upper India, into which the Sanskrit was introduced by conquerors from other kingdoms in some very remote age”

tamiḻiya moḻikaḷiliruntu vētamoḻi, camaṟkirutam vaṭa intiya (intō āriya moḻik kuṭumpattiṉavākak karutappaṭum) moḻikaḷ, ākiyavaṟṟil ēṟiyuḷḷa oliyaṉiyal, ilakkaṇaviyal moḻiyiyal kūṟukaḷ palavaṟṟi emaṉō tamatu 1956 kaṭṭuriyil niṟuviṉār.  

oliyaṉiyal : ṭa;ḷa (rikvētattil), ṉa ākiya vaḷi nāvolikaḷ (retroflex/cerebral/domal tamiḻiya moḻi kaḷiliruntu kaṭaṉ peṟṟavi. avvolikaḷik koṇṭa coṟkaḷit tamiḻiliruntu kaṭaṉ peṟṟa piṉṉar, olikaḷiyum vaṇṇamāliyil cērttaṉar.
viṉiyeccam (gerund/absolutive/incomplete verb/ past nonfinite verb/converb/indeclinable participle/ adverbial participle/past participle (Jothimuthu), conjunctive participle (Grierson)  rikvēta kālattilēyē ēṟiviṭṭatu.
nēr kūṟṟu muṭintavuṭaṉ “eṉa” eṉṟu tamiḻil varuvatu pōl (“taṉceyviṉi muṭitteṉak kēṭpal-” puṟanāṉūṟu 27:9-10) camaṟkirutattil kūṟṟukkup piṉṉar “iti” iti (= ippaṭik kūṟiṉār) eṉa varutal.
etirolic coṟkaḷ (echo words ) “puli kili” pōṉṟavi
olikkuṟippuc coṟkaḷ - caḷacaḷa, paṭa paṭa pōṉṟavi.
ōṭu, ōṭṭu, ōṭṭuvi; naṭa, naṭattu, naṭattuvi pōṉṟa vāypāṭṭu (simplex, cusative, cusative of cusative) viṉi vaṭivaṅkaḷ.
emeṉō 1974 kaṭṭuri; tamiḻ um payaṉpāṭu pōṉṟa camaṟkiruta api api ai payaṉpaṭuttutal.
mēlatu kaṭṭuri: tirāviṭa uṟavu muṟic coṟkaḷilum cari intō āriya uṟavumuṟic coṟkaḷilum cari perumpālum toḻilik kuṟikkum cātippeyar āṇukku uḷḷatu. āṉāl peṇṇik kuṟikkum poḻutu intac cātikkāraṉuṭiya makaḷ/maṉivi/ tāy/piṟa uṟavu eṉṟa aḷavilēyē amintu viṭukiṟatu.

mēṟkaṇṭa ceytikaḷi kāliṉ mācikā tamatu Defining a Linguistic Area : South Asia (1976) nūlil virivāka viḷakki yuḷḷār. tamiḻiya moḻikaḷi yoṭṭiya māṟṟaṅkaḷē intō āriya moḻikaḷil ēṟiyuḷḷaṉa eṉpatu avar karuttu “(India) is a fairly stable typological area where the brunt of the burden of adaptation is borne by intrusive rather than local languages.”

pāvāṇaruṭiya vaṭamoḻi varalāṟu (1967) 295-321 pakkaṅkaḷil ilakkaṇavatikāram eṉṉum pakutiyil piṉvarum ilakkaṇa/ moḻiyiyal kūṟukaḷi vētamoḻiyum camaṟkirutamum tamiḻiya moḻikaḷiṭamiruntē kaṭaṉpeṟṟuḷḷa eṉat teḷivāka niṟuvukiṟār.

aḷapu 6. eḻuttuvaṭivam 7. puṇarcci.


colliyal

“vaṭamoḻi vēṟṟumiyamippum tamiḻit taḻuviyatu” “vaṭamoḻi tirimoḻiyātaliṉ ataṉ viṉikaṭku vērc coṟkaḷ ammoḻiyil illi. aviperumpālum iyalmoḻiyākiya tamiḻil tāṉ uḷḷaṉa.” (tātu pāṭa vērkaḷiṉ taṉmi paṟṟi ēṟkeṉevē mēlē iyal 1l, kaṇṭōm). kuṟippuc coṟkaḷ - acikaḷ; iṇippiṭic col (um - untu Skt - utta; OE, Eng and) cuṭṭu/ viṉāccoṟkaḷ, viṉiyeccam.

toṭariyal

tokic col (samāsa); coṟṟoṭar varici (“perumpālum tamiḻ muṟiyi ottatē”)

The Primary Classicial Language of the World  pakkaṅkaḷ 279 - 286 l tamiḻiliruntu intō - airōppiya moḻikaḷil ēṟiyuḷḷa (muṉṉoṭṭukaḷ, piṉṉoṭṭukaḷ, cuṭṭukaḷ, viṉi īṟukaḷ mutaliya) mēlum pala moḻiyiyal, ilakkaṇak kūṟukaḷik kuṟippiṭṭuḷḷār. 

moḻi paṭippaṭiyāka uruvāṉatā?
allatu tiṭumeṉa uruvāṉatā?

tiṭumeṉattāṉ eṉṟu 1783 l karutiyavar ṭākṭar jāṉcaṉ :

moḻi oruvar allatu oru ciṟu kuḻuviṉariṭam tiṭumeṉat tōṉṟiyirukka vēṇṭum. āyiram kuḻantikaḷō allatu ēṉ pattu laṭcam kuḻantikaḷ cērntō moḻiyi uruvākkiyirukka iyalātu. kuralvaḷiyum olikkum uṟuppukaḷum ataṟkēṟṟa pakkuva niliyil irukkum kuḻantip paruvattil ataṟkāṉa aṟivu kuḻantikaḷiṭam irātu; aṟivuvaḷarvataṟkuḷ kuralvaḷiyum piṟa uṟuppukaḷum pēccolikaḷi olikka iyalāta nili eytiviṭum.

It must have come by inspiration. A thousand, nay a million of children could not invent a language. While the organs are pliable there is not understanding enough to form a language; by the time there is understanding enough the organs are become stiff.
hāri hāyjar karuttum atuvē: [moḻikku muntiya niliyiliruntu (olikkuṟippukaḷ pōṉṟavi) moḻi tōṉṟiyataṉāl] mānta iṉa varalāṟṟil niṉittuppārkka iyalāta eṇṇiṟanta puraṭcikaramāṉa māṟṟaṅkaḷ nikaḻntaṉa. attakiya uruvākkam orē oru muṟi tāṉ orē iṭattil tāṉ nikaḻntirukka iyalum. uruvāṉatum atu kaṭum virivil māntarkaḷ aṉivariṭamum paraviyirukkum atāvatu ñāla mutaṉmoḻi ōriṭattil oru muṟiuruvāṉatē; mānta marapiyaliṉ paṭi aṉittu māntarum uṭaṉ piṟappiṉar, atē pōl mutaṉmoḻiyaṭippaṭiyilum uṭaṉpiṟappukaḷē yāvar.”

[The cirucumstances under which pre - language could have become language are] so far - reaching. so revolutionary and so fruitful as to suggest that it was created only once and in only in one place. Once created. it would have spread to all hominids irresistibly and speedily. It suggests, in other words the unitary origin of language, and the unitary origin of language suggests in turn,. that men are cultural as well as genetic and biological brothers Harry Hoijer (1969) “The origin of language”.

ñālamutaṉmoḻi oṉṟē eṉpati valiyuṟutti māris cuvātecu 1972 l The Origin and Diversification of Language eṉṟa virivāṉa nūl eḻutiyuḷḷār.

ār.em. ṭapiḷyū. ṭikcaṉ The rise and fall of languages (1977) nūlil ñāla mutaṉmoḻitiṭumeṉa, veṭi veṭittatu pōlat (like an explosion) tōṉṟiyirukkum eṉkiṟār. mānta iṉa mūḷivaḷarcci takka takuti peṟṟa niliyil māntamūḷi tiṭumeṉak kaṭṭukkōppāṉa oru muḻu amippāka moḻiyit tōṟṟuvittirukkum eṉkiṟār(The human brin would have invented it, almost as a complete system; “Language would have burst forth”) tamiḻ oṉṟē iyalmoḻi eṉṟum eṉavē ammoḻiyil tāṉ coṟkaḷ (ēṉ piṟa moḻic coṟkaḷum) aṉittum cila aṭippaṭi vērkaḷiliruntu ēraṇa muṟippaṭi paṭippaṭiyāka aṟivupūrvamāka ēṟkattakka vakiyil vaḷarntu peruki vantuḷḷataṉi meyppikka muṭiyum eṉṟa pāvāṇar kōṭpāṭṭukku ṭikcaṉ karuttu oraḷavu ātaravu taruvatik kāṇalām. [“iyalmoḻi eṉṟu pāvāṇar kūṟiyatu mānta iṉa mutal tāymoḻikku mika neruṅkiyatu tamiḻ eṉṟa
karuttil.]”
10. moḻi tiṭumeṉa uruvākiyatāyiṉ. ñāṉap pirakācar (1927) mutaliṟ kūṟiyatum pāvāṇar(1943,1950,1953,1967 nūl pakkam 70-83 tiṭpamāka virivāka vaṭivamittatum āṉa cuṭṭaṭik koḷkiyāṉatu ñāṉamutal moḻi āyvāḷar karututaṟku uriyatu. tamiḻc coṟkaḷuḷ mukkāṟ pakuti cuṭṭaṭic coṟkaḷiliruntu uruvākiyatu eṉpār pāvāṇar (1950). cuṭṭaṭik koḷkiyi mikac curukkamāka piṉvarumāṟu taralām.

(ā - ī - ū) ----------------   (  a - i - u)
a. cēymik karuttuc coṟkaḷ
i. aṉmi, piṉmi, iḻiki
u (muṉmic cuṭṭu) ul maṟṟum ukarattuṭaṉ k,c,t,n,p,m ākiya meykaḷic cērttu(Plenary operation of the principle of consonental prosthesis) uruvākum ul, kul, cul, tul, (nul), pul, mul ākiya mūla vērkaḷil iruntu tōṉṟal (muṉvarutal) muṉmi, muṟcellal (cellutal), neruṅkal, (ceṟittal, kūṭutal); poruntutal (kūṭutal, ottal), vaḷital, vaḷittal, turuvutal ākiya eṭṭup peruṅkaruttukkaḷum. (ivaṟṟiṟku iṭippaṭṭaṉavum ivaṟṟiṟkiḷittaṉavumāṉa) ettuṇiyō nuṇkaruttukkaḷum avaṟṟiṟkāṉa eṇṇiṟanta tamiḻc coṟkaḷum avaṟṟiṉ paṉmaṭittiripukaḷāṉa palvēṟu “kuṭumpa” moḻic coṟkaḷum piṟakkiṉṟaṉa eṉpār avar. ikkoḷkiyiṉ virivip pāvāṇar mutaltāymoḻi (1953) nūlil tantuḷḷār. 1979 l pāvāṇar eḻutiya “An epitome of the Lemurian Language and its ramifications”eṉṉum āṅkilak kaṭṭuriyil ikkaruttiṉ āṅkilac curukkatti piṉvarumāṟu tantuḷḷār:

“The three fundamental ideas, viz., remoteness, proximity, and frontality (or forwardness) expressed by the three demonstrative vowels have generated a million words which go to make up Tamil, and have also produced subsequently and genealogically a confused multiplicity of dependant languages of varying degrees of richness and importance, through the process of mutation and derivation.... Of the words contined in any language, the greatest majority owe their origin to the idea of frontwardness.”

ec. es. ṭēviṭ (1966) kaṭṭurikaḷil kuṟippiṭum 21 aṭippaṭit tamiḻvērkaḷum (vaḷ/vaṇ, uḷ (uṇ) uṇṭu, kēḷ/ kēḷir, kēṇmi, koḷ/ koṭu/kōṭal; kīḻ; pōḻ; ol, kal, kāl, cāl, nil, pāl, palcil, tol val, eḷ, oḷ, viḷ) avi cārnta coṟṟokutikaḷum oru pāṉmi pāvāṇar karuttōṭu ottu nōkkattakkaṉa vākum.

māntaṉmoḻi (ēṟattāḻa 50000 āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉar) orē iṭattil tiṭumeṉat tōṉṟiyirukkalāmeṉṟum atilikuntu paṭippaṭiyāka urumāṟit tōṉṟiyaviyē iṉṟuḷḷa 6000 moḻikaḷum eṉakkarutum aṟiñar karuttukaḷi mēlē kaṇṭōm. inta iṭattil tamiḻakattil palarum iṉṟu ceppivarum oruātāramaṟṟa kūṟṟik kuṟippiṭa vēṇṭum. atu eṉṉa? nōvām cāmaskiyum ‘ulaka moḻikaḷ aṉittiṟkum tamiḻmoḻiyē tāyāka irukkakkūṭum eṉṟu kūṟiviṭṭār’ eṉpatē atu. appaṭi avar kūṟavē illi. kūṟakkūṭiyavarum allar. camaṇamuṉivar ūṉ cōṟu ceytār eṉpatu pōla uḷḷatu cāmski paṟṟiya ikkūṟṟu!’avar āyvuppulam vēṟu; māntaṉiṭam enta uḷaviyal - uṭaliyal aṭippaṭiyil moḻi āṟṟal tōṉṟiyirukkalām eṉpatē avar āyvup pulamākum; varalāṟṟu moḻiyiyal pakkamō, ñāla mutaṉmoḻi etuvāka irukkalām eṉpatu paṟṟiyō vaṇṇaṉi moḻiyiyalaṟiñarāṉa avar celpavarallar. mēlum cāmski pōṉṟōr tākkattiṉāl tāṉ varalāṟṟu moḻiyiyal cārnta āyvukaḷiṉ kaṭanta muppatu nāṟpatu āṇṭukaḷil ñāla mutaṉmoḻi āyvukaḷ pōṉṟavaṟṟil tēkka nili ēṟpaṭṭatu eṉpati viḷakkuvar āṇṭrūs kārsṭārs - makkārti pōṉṟōr. tamiḻil iruntu ulakamoḻikaḷ aṉittum tōṉṟiṉa eṉṟu cāmski kūṟiṉār eṉṟa piḻicceyti eppaṭip paraviyatu. 22.11.2001 Frontline  itaḻil cāmski kalkattāvil pēciyatu veḷivantatu. āṅkila aṟivu nirampāta varum moḻiyiyal paṟṟit teriyātavarum āṉoru aṉpar ōmcakti ēpral 2002 itaḻil antap pēccit tamiḻil tamiḻākkam ceyta poḻutu ippiḻik karutati acciṭṭār cāmsaki pēcciṉ cariyāṉa tamiḻākkamum āṅkila mūlamum aṭuttuta tarappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. valuvāṉa ātāraṅkaḷ irukkum poḻutu kaṟpaṉic ceytikaḷik kūṟuvatu kaṉiyiruppak kāykavar vatākum. [ulakil uḷḷa 6000 moḻikaḷil 3340 moḻikaḷil ovvoṉṟiyum pēcuvōr pattāyirattukkumkuṟivāṉavarkaḷē. avaṟṟuḷ 3000 moḻikaḷ 2100kkuḷ aḻintuviṭum eṉṟu moḻiyiyalaṟiñar karutukiṉṟaṉar. itu teriyāmal “aḻivuṟum niliyil uḷḷa moḻikaḷ paṭṭiyalil 6 kōṭi makkaḷ pēcum tamiḻiyum yuṉeskō cērttuḷḷatu” eṉṟa pētimik karuttip palarum parappalāyiṉar. itu muḻut tavaṟu eṉpatu. toṉmic cemmoḻi tamiḻ 2007 nūlil iṟuti iyalil teḷivākkap paṭṭuḷḷatu. atu pōlavēcāmaski paṟṟiya piḻikkaruttiyum teḷivākkuvatu iṉṟiyamiyātatu. cāmskiyiṭam yārāvatu eḻutik kēṭṭāl ‘eṉṉatu? nāṉā? eppoḻutu coṉṉēṉ” eṉṟu allavā kūṟi nakippar.]

ulakamoḻikaḷ aṉittiṟkum tamiḻē tāy moḻiyāka irukkakkūṭum eṉṟu iṉṟuvari mēṉāṭṭu moḻiyiyalaṟiñar evarum kūṟavilli. kālṭuvel 1856/1875l kūṟiyatum ñālamutaṉmoḻikkut tol tamiḻ (tirāviṭam) mika neruṅkiyatāka irukkalām eṉpatē. (vivaraṅkaḷē mēlē kaṇṭōm) ñāla mutaṉmoḻi tamiḻ eṉṟa mākaṟal kārttikēya mutaliyār, ñāṉappirakācar, tēvanēyap pāvāṇar koḷki iṉi ulakil virivil niṟuvap paṭalām. -- iḷiñarāṉa moḻiyiyalaṟiñar (tamiḻōṭu ēṉiya kuṭumpamoḻi oṉṟiraṇṭil āḻnta pulami peṟṟavar) mēli nāṭukaḷil iṉṟu ivvāyvukaḷil īṭupaṭuvōruṭaṉ oruṅkiṇintu pāvāṇar vaḻiyil āyvuceytāl. 

nōvām cāmski 22.11.01 aṉṟu kalkattāp palkalikkaḻakattil āṟṟiya uri [āṅkila mūlam

Frontline  21.12.01] moḻi paṟṟiya aṟiviyal uṇmikaḷ.

(“kalkattāp palkalik kaḻakam tarum”) inta muṉivar paṭṭatti intiyāvil peṟuvatu eṉakkup perumakiḻcci tarukiṟatu. intiyāvil tāṉ nāṉ cārntirukkum “moḻiyiyal” eṉṉum viññāṉap pulattil kaṇicamāṉa pakuti cumār 2000 āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar uruvākkap paṭṭatu eṉpatu uṅkaḷukkum teriyum. ippoḻutu nām ‘toṭariyal ilakkaṇam’ (generative grammar) eṉṟuaḻikkum vakiyic cārnta mutalāvatu ilakkaṇameṉṟu oraḷavukkuk kūṟattakkatu pāṇiṉiyiṉ
camcukkiruta ilakkaṇam tāṉ. 50 āṇṭukaḷukku muṉ eṉatu putiya ‘toṭariyal ilakkaṇak koḷkiyi nāṉ niṟuviya poḻutu pāṇiṉiyattilum itiyotta cila karuttukkaḷ iruntatu teriyātu, antapputukkoḷki moḻiyiyalil vērūṉṟiya piṉṉar tāṉ paḻiya ilakkaṇa marapukaḷilum irunta atupōṉṟa karuttukkaḷ iṉam kāṇappaṭṭu iṉṟiya moḻiyiyalil viññāṉa aṭippaṭiyil putu viyākkiyāṉaṅkaḷ tarappaṭṭaṉa. paṇṭiya ilakkaṇac celvaṅkaḷ palavaṟṟil (pāṇiṉīyam uṭpaṭa) ikkaruttukkaḷ aṭiyāḷam kāṇappaṭṭaṉa. eṉiṉum appaḻiya nūlkaḷiṉ kūṟṟukkaḷil iṉṉum teḷivu peṟa vēṇṭiyaṉa pala uḷḷaṉa; mēlum ārāycci ceytāl naṟpayaṉ kiṭṭum. 
itaṟkiṭiyil taṟkāla moḻiyiyal āyvukaḷ taṉittuvamāṉa pātiyil celkiṉṟaṉa. maṉitaṉiṉ uṭaṟkūṟṟiyal cūḻaliṉ oru paṅkākavē moḻiyi avvāyvukaḷ karutukiṉṟaṉa. “uṭaṟkūṟṟiyalcār nōkku” (biolinguistic approach) eṉpatu itaṟku oru peyar ākum. uṭaliṉ, kuṟippāka mūḷiyiṉ oru āṟṟal eṉṟē moḻi karutappaṭukiṟatu. kaṇkaḷiṉ pārkkum āṟṟal, taci narampukaḷiṉ iyaṅkiyal āṟṟal pōṉṟatutāṉ mūḷiyiṉ moḻi āṟṟal eṉpatē innōkku.

maṉita iṉattukkēyuriya “moḻi āṟṟal” iraṇṭu vakiyil taṉittaṉmi peṟṟatu. 1.atu maṉita iṉattil aṉivarukkum oru cīrāka amintuḷḷatu. 2. uyiriṉaṅkaḷil maṉitaṉukku maṭṭumē moḻi uriyatu.

ciṟappiyalpu I cila āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar vari caṟṟu viyappuṭaṉēyē karutappaṭṭu vantatu. ippoḻutu appaṭi alla. moḻi āṟṟalil maṉita iṉattiṉ palvēṟu pirivukaḷukkuḷ entavēṟupāṭum illi eṉpati aṇmi āyvukaḷ kāṭṭu kiṉṟaṉa. iṉṟikkuc cumār 1,00,000 āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar irunta āṇ- peṇ tampatiyariṉ ( allatu oru cila āṭavar – makaḷir kuḻuviṉ) vāricukaḷē iṉṟu ulakil vāḻum aṉittu (600 kōṭi) maṉitarkaḷum eṉṟa muṭivip potuvāka ellā viññāṉikaḷum ēṟkiṉṟaṉar. itiliruntu eṉṉa terikiṟatu? vaḷamāṉa ceṟivāṉa moḻittiṟaṉi aṭivataṟkāṉa iyalpāṉa āṟṟal aṉivarukkum camamākavē amintuḷḷatu. avvāṟṟalil vēṟupāṭu irukkumāyiṉ atu mikamikac ciṟiya vēṟupāṭēyākum. iṉṟuḷḷa muṟikaḷiṉ paṭi avvēṟupāṭṭik kaṇṭupiṭikkavē muṭiyātu.

ciṟappiyalpu II viyappiṟkuriyatu. maṉitaṉ tavira vēṟu enta uyiriṉattukkum “moḻi” yeṉṟu kuṟippiṭattakka āṟṟal illavē illi. oru cila pūcciyiṉaṅkaḷiṭiyē (tēṉī, eṟumpu) maṭṭum moḻiyiṉ taṉmiyil oru nuṇṇiya paṅku oruvēḷi irukkalām. maṉitaṉ tavira ovvoru uyiriṉamum tattamakkuḷ toṭarpu koḷḷum muṟikaḷip paṭṭiyaliṭṭāl atil “moḻi” illi! “moḻi eṉpatu aṭippaṭiyil ceytip parimāṟṟak karuvi” eṉṟa potuvāṉa nampikkiyum ātāram aṟṟatēyākum.

maṉita iṉattiṉ piṟa taṉmikaḷip pōl “moḻi” eṉpatiyum parampari marapiyal(genetics) tāṉ nirṇayikkiṟatu. avaravar vāḻkki aṉupavattiṟkēṟṟapaṭi moḻittiṟaṉ vaḷarcci vēṟupaṭalā meṉiṉum vēṟupāṭukaḷ varampukkuṭpaṭṭavi. maṉitaṉākap piṟantavuṭaṉēyē aṉivarukkum moḻittiṟaṉi vaḷarttuk koḷḷum aṭippaṭi āṟṟal. orē mātiri amintuviṭukiṟatu. eṉavē tāṉ “vēṟupāṭukaḷ varampukkuṭpaṭṭavi” eṉṟa nili. maṉitaṉiṉ moḻikaḷ (nēṟṟiya, iṉṟiya, nāḷiya moḻikaḷ) aṉittum ēṟattāḻa orē accil vārttaviyē. nām vilaṅkukaḷip paṟṟi āyvu ceykiṟōm. cevvāyk kiraka uyiriyāṉa (cevvāyil uyiriṉamē kiṭiyātu! nām colvatu kaṟpaṉi uyiri!) oṉṟu iṅku vantu maṉitaṉi āyvu ceytāl “maṉita iṉam muḻuvatum orē moḻiyittāṉ pēcukiṟatu - cila vēṟupāṭukaḷuṭaṉ” eṉṟu tāṉ kūṟum. nam moḻikaḷiṭiyē uḷḷa vēṟupāṭukaḷ aṉṟāṭa vāḻkiyil mukkiyam āṉavi. āṉāl aviyaṉittiṭamum uḷḷa aṭippaṭi orumippāṭṭi (atu eṉṉa eṉṟu putti pūrvamākap purintu koḷḷāviṭṭālum) uṇarvu pūrvamāka uṭkoṇṭutāṉ ellā maṉitarum ceyalpaṭukiṉṟaṉar. moḻikaḷik kaṟpikkap payaṉpaṭum marapilakkaṇaṅkaḷum akaramutalikaḷum moḻikku moḻi uḷḷa taṟceyalāka vāytta vēṟupāṭukaḷic cuṭṭik kāṭṭukiṉṟaṉa. avaṟṟiṉ nōkkattil atu caritāṉ. viññāṉa muṟiyil moḻiyi āyvu ceyvatil oṉṟuk koṉṟu toṭarpuṭiya iraṇṭu amcaṅkaḷ uḷḷaṉa. 1. eṉṟum māṟāta oli, poruḷ, ilakkaṇa amippu variccaṭṭaṅkaḷmaṉita mūḷiyiṉ amippilēyē nirṇayikkappaṭṭu viṭukiṉṟaṉa. 2. ovvoruvarum tam aṉupavattiṟkēṟpa eytum moḻittiṟaṉum (ciṟcila vēṟupāṭukaḷ iruppiṉum) anta māṟāta nirṇayattiṟkuḷ tāṉ vērūṉṟi irukkum.

maṉitaṉiṉ mūḷiyil moḻittiṟaṉ māṟāta vaṇṇam nirṇayikkappaṭṭu viṭṭa niliyil ciṟu aḷavilāṉa māṟu pāṭukaḷukku maṭṭumē vāyppu (options) uṇṭu. ivaṟṟiṉ taṉmikaḷi āyvu ceytapiṉ ceyya vēṇṭiyatu māṟupāṭṭu vāyppu oṉṟit tērnteṭuttāl oru moḻiyum, vēṟu oṉṟit tērnteṭuttāl vēṟu oru moḻiyum oru maṉitaṉiṉ moḻiyāka amintuviṭukiṉṟaṉa eṉṟu kāṭṭuvatutāṉ. oru iṭattil, oru kālattil avvāyppu tamiḻ eṉṟa moḻiyāka amintuviṭukiṟatu. kaṟārākac coṉṉāl tamiḻiṉ vaṭṭāra vaḻakkukaḷil oṉṟu eṉac colla vēṇṭum [atāvatu tamiḻnāṭṭil piṟakkum kuḻanti tamiḻ pēcukiṟatu; atuvum vaṭṭārattukkut takkavāṟu attamiḻiṉ taṉmiyum vēṟupaṭukiṟatu eṉṟu colla varukiṟār cāmski]. iṉṉoru iṭam, kālattil avvāyppu “cuvāhili” moḻi ākiviṭukiṟatu. ēṉiya moḻikaḷukkum avvāṟē kūṟik koḷka. intat tattuvatti vēṟu coṟkaḷil piṉvarumāṟu kūṟulām. maṉita moḻiyiṉ nirṇayikkappaṭṭu viṭṭa aṭippaṭi, ciṟu ciṟu māṟupāṭṭu vāyppukaḷ, ivaṟṟiṉ aṭippaṭiyil ovvoru moḻiyilum ettaṉi vākkiyaṅkaḷiyum col – coṟṟoṭar kaḷiyum amikkalām? avaṟṟiṉ  li, poruḷ eṉṉa? karuttit terivikka, takavalkōra, kati colla, pōṉṟa pala kāriyaṅkaḷukku ententa vaḻikaḷil ammoḻiyil vākkiyaṅkaḷiyum coṟ ṟoṭarkaḷiyum amikkac cāttiyamuṇṭu? ivaṟṟik kaṇṭu piṭippatē moḻiyiyaliṉ vēli. itu eḷitalla. kaṭiṉamāṉatu. varuṅkālattil pala talimuṟi āyvāḷarkaḷiṉ āṟṟalum muyaṟciyum ivvāyvukkut tēvi eṉpatil aiyamilli. eṉiṉum āyvu vaḷarcci ūkkam taruvatāka uḷḷatu. kaṭanta 20 āṇṭukaḷil māṟupaṭṭa ilakkaṇakkaṭṭamippuk koṇṭa moḻikaḷ palavaṟṟip paṟṟiya uṇmikaḷ pala teriyavantuḷḷaṉa. muṉṉarē āyvukkuṭpaṭuttappaṭṭa (camacukkirutam pōṉṟa) moḻikaḷ aḷavukku, immoḻikaḷilāṉa putiya āyvukaḷum āḻamākanaṭantuḷḷaṉa. viyattaku moḻiyiyal uṇmikaḷ kaṇṭupiṭikkappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa. niṉittuk kūṭap pārkkāta viṉākkaḷ eḻuntuḷḷaṉa. palavaṟṟukkup poruttamāṉa viṭikaḷ tōṉṟip putiya ārāyccip pātikaḷukku vaḻi vakukkappaṭṭuḷḷatu. 

aṇmiyil tōṉṟiyuḷḷa oru āyvuppulattil eṉakkum ārvam uṇṭu. moḻiyāṉatu laṭciya vaṭivamippu (optional design) koṇṭatā? eṉpatu atu.puṟaccūḻniliyiṉ nipantaṉikaḷukkuṭpaṭṭē moḻiyip payaṉpaṭutta muṭiyum. (e.kā. nēraṭiyākavō karuvi mūlamākavō uṇarvuppulaṉukku Sensorimotor system eṭṭuvatāka moḻi irukka vēṇṭum) annipantaṉikaḷukkut tīrvāka amiyak kūṭiyatāka enta aḷavukku maṉitaṉiṉ moḻi irukkiṟatu eṉpatē inta āyvuppulam. “iyaṟki muḻumipeṟṟatu” eṉṟu uḷḷuṇarvu pūrvamāka uṇarntu coṉṉāṉ kalīliyō. moḻi itaṟkup  porunti varukiṟatā eṉa nirūpikkumāṟu nām kēṭkalām. navīṉa viññāṉak karuttukkaḷ palavaṟṟilum inta uḷḷuṇarvuk karuttu vaḻi kāṭṭiyāka iruppatāl iti nirūpikkum paṇi viññāṉikaḷ mēṟkoḷḷa vēṇṭiya paṇitāṉ. aṟpa uyiriṉaṅkaḷip poṟuttavariyil attakiya viṉākkaḷukkuc cila viṭikaḷ  kaṇṭupiṭikkappaṭṭuḷṉa. viras nuṇkirumikaḷiṉ puṟapparappu ‘pala camapakkappaṭṭi vaṭivam’ (polyhedral) koṇṭatu etaṉāl eṉpatik kaṇṭupiṭittu viṭṭaṉar. moḻiyiṉ taṉmikaḷukkum itu pōṉṟa viḷakkam kiṭikkalām eṉṟu nampakkūṭiya āyvukaḷ naṭipeṟṟu varukiṉṟaṉa. uyiriṉappariṇāma vaḷarcciyil
kaṭiciyāka aṇmiyil tōṉṟiyavaṉ maṉitaṉ. ammaṉita iṉattil maṭṭumē tōṉṟiyuḷḷa (maṉita uṭal - uḷaviyal veḷip pāṭāṉa) moḻiyāṉatu maṉita iyalpilum vāḻvilum miyappakuti vakukkiṟatu. itu cārnta aṇmi āyvuk kaṇṭupiṭippukaḷ pala poruttamāṉaviyāka uḷḷaṉa. avi potuvāka uyiriṉap pariṇāma vaḷarcci kuṟittum kuṟippāka maṉita iṉam kuṟittum pala viṉākkaḷi eḻuppukiṉṟaṉa. moḻi, ati maṉitaṉ eytippayaṉ paṭuttuma muṟikaḷ, maṉita vāḻvil ataṉ paṅku paṟṟiyellām neṭuṅkālamāka eḻuntu vantuḷḷa pala piracciṉikaḷukku ittakiya   āyvukaḷ putiya viyattaku tīrvukaḷit tantuḷḷaṉa.

moḻipaṟṟiyum mūḷiyiṉ ēṉiya uyarnilic ceyal pāṭukaḷ paṟṟiyum āṉa āyvukaḷil varum āṇṭukaḷil paraparappāṉa āyvu muṭivukaḷ veḷivaralām. iṉṟu nuṭpamāṉa cikkalāṉa viññāṉattuṟiyāka vaḷarntuvarum ‘moḻiyiyal’ āyvukaḷil mukkiyamāka āyvup pātikaḷ cilavaṟṟukkup paṇṭik kālattilēyē kālkōḷ ceyyappaṭṭa inta (intiya) nāṭṭiviṭa vēṟu enta iṭam poruttamāṉatu ivvāyvukḷit toṭara! eṉṉip poṟutta vari ittakiya moḻiyiyal āyvukaḷik kūrntu nōkkavum, avaṟṟil paṅku peṟavum ārvamāṉa etirpārppuṭaṉ nāṉ kāttirukkiṟēṉ.
 
Understanding Human Language

Address by professor Noam chonsky at the special convocation of the University of Calcutta held on November 22, 2001 to award the degree D.Litt - Honoris Causa.

I would like to express my gratitude at being welcomed into this distinguished intellectual community, with its vibrant and rich tradition. It is particularly gratifying to receive this honour in India. My professional  field, as I am sure you know, was in large part created in India, 2,500 years ago. The first “generative grammar” in something like the modern sense is Panini’s grammar of Sanskrit. Nothing was known about these similarities at the origins of the modern versions 50 years ago. It was only after the modern field had taken shape that earlier tradition, long forgotten, began to be explored and reinterpreted in the light of recent insights. Many treasures were discovered, among them Panini’s classic – though crucial issues of interpretation remin obscure, and there are surely research topics that could prove highly rewarding. 

Meanwhile contemporary inquiries proceeded along their own distinctive course. Characteristically, they view language in a biological setting, adopting what is sometimes called a “biolinguistic approach”. From this point of view, the human faculty of language is regarded basically as an organ of the body, mostly the brin, more or less on a par with the visual system or the system of motor organization.

The language faculty is a “species property” in a dual sense. First, it is close to uniform for the species; second, it is apparently unique to humans in essentials. 

The first of these properties is less surprising than it seemed a few years ago. Recent studies indicate that there is remarkably little genetic variation within the human species, so little that it is now commonly assumed that contemporary humans are all descendants of a very small breeding group, perhaps about 100,000 years ago, a conclusion that has broad implications, With regard to language, apart from the margins, variation in the capacity to acquire a rich and highly articulated knowledge of language is so slight as to be virtually undetectable, at least by present means.

The uniqueness property is more surprising. There are no known analogues to human language elsewhere in the animal world. The closest analogies, and these are very weak, are remote: perhaps in some species of insects. Human language does not even find a place in standard taxonomies of animal communication systems; and in fact, there is no strong reason to think of it as primarily a system of communication, contrary to common belief.

Language is like other biological systems, however, in that its basic character is genetically determined. Each person, of course, undergoes a specific course of development, shaped by individual experience, but in highly limited ways. The outcomes are largely a result of shared initial endowment. The human languages, existing or possible, are pretty much cast to the same mould. A rational Martian scientist, studying humans the way we study other animals, could reasonably conclude that there really is only one language, with only minor variations. The variations are very important for our lives; the far deeper uniformities we simply take for granted, without awareness. Similarly, traditional and pedagogical grammars and dictionaries are concerned with the unpredictable and somewhat accidental variation, rightly for their special purposes. The interests of the scientific study of language are virtually complementary: the invariant principles of sound, meaning, and structure that are  rooted in our mental nature and that determine the fundamental nature of the languages that each person comes to acquire under normal circumstances. 

One basic problem, then, is to discover the invariant principles of the language faculty and the limited options of variation, and then to show that the possible human languages are determined by selecting among the options: one choice yields Tamil (more exactly, a specific variety of Tamil) another yields Swahili, etc. Putting it differently, the task is to show that with a specific choice of options by adhering to the fixed principles one can literally deduce the infinite array of expressions of language; their sound, their meaning, the ways in which they can be used to express thoughts, to request information, to tell stories, and numerous others. The task is immensely challenging and difficult. It will doubtless occupy the efforts and energies of many generations of researchers. Nevertheless, there has been quite encouraging progress. In the past 20 years particularly, there has been a flood of discoveries about languages of virtually every known typological variety. Like the well – studied languages, these have been investigated in far greater depth than ever before, revealing many entirely unexpected properties. New questions have arisen that had never been envisioned before. In many cases there have also been plusible answers, sometimes opening new directions for inquiry. 

One novel question that has come to the fore in recent years, and that happens to be of particular interest to me, is the question of “optimal design”. To what extent is human language an optimal solution to externally – imposed conditions that language much satisfy to be usable at all (for example, accessibility to sensori motor systems). Equivalently, we may ask to what extent language satisfies the Galilean intuition that “nature is perfect” and it is the task of the scientist to prove it, a guiding intuition for the modern sciences.

There are some answers to such questions for very simple organisms: for example, an explanation of the fact that the shells to viruses are polyhedral. Current work suggests that something similar may be true for human language, a biological system that has emerged in the last moment of evolutionary time, in the most complex organism known, and is surely at the core of our nature and life. Such conclusions, which by now have considerable substance, rise many questions about biological evolution and development generally, and about the human species in particular. These research efforts have also provided new and often surprising solutions to long- standing problems,\ of language, its acquisition and use, and its place in the biological world.

There should be very exciting years ahead in the study of language and other higher mental faculties. There is no better place to pursue these questions than in the land that was the original home of some of the major strands of inquiry that are now being woven into a most intriguing fabric. Speaking personally, I look forward with much eagerness and anticipation to observing, and participating in, these very promising developments.
Front line, December 21, 2001.
